## D-039 — STOREFRONT-READ-001: the first real Storefront read — server-rendered live read path, `public.storefront_menu`, profile/media schema, browse-only live mode

- **Status:** **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9), implemented **locally** under Work ID **STOREFRONT-READ-001** from the sealed integration plan (`worktrees/output/storefront-read-001-plan-20260923T152536Z/`, outside git). The human owner (Saleh) took the plan's decisions **D0, D1, D2 and D13** on 2026-09-23 with the implementation authorization; the remaining plan decisions are taken at their recommended defaults and named below. **Not yet ratified:** pending independent implementation review and human approval at the merge gate. **Nothing is applied to hosted** by this decision: `supabase/migrations/20260923120000_storefront_read_001.sql` is local-only until a separately approved apply preceded by the [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §16 drift watch; no provider setting, build or deployment changes.
- **Context:** STOREFRONT-UI-001 delivered the approved customer experience as a static export over typed fixtures; STOREFRONT-HOST-001 hosts one protected fixture deployment (`dpl_4bCrhnW4in27xQYtTcekx63xcBea`) as immutable evidence. A real restaurant menu cannot be a static export: tenant slugs are data, a menu changes during the day, and the publishable key must never reach a browser document. D-037 (SEC-001) left the `anon` allowlist **empty** so that the first public read would be added deliberately, by name, with its own proof. The read-slice security work the untracked INFRA plans called "SEC-002" had no ticket; owner decision **D0** executes it inside this implementation under independent review.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Read topology (D2, D13).** The Storefront is a **server-rendered** Next.js application: `output: 'export'` is removed; the tenant routes `/s/[slug]/*` under the four locale roots are dynamic with `revalidate = 60` and `expireTime = 360` (served `Cache-Control: s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300`); the server reads the database over the **same-origin server boundary** — global `fetch` from server code, `STOREFRONT_SUPABASE_URL` / `STOREFRONT_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` read only inside server code at request time (D8) — with **no** BFF, proxy, middleware, edge function or browser-side database client (`connect-src 'self'` stays). Every future Storefront build, fixture mode included, is a server build; the immutable HOST-001 deployment remains the fixture evidence, and fixture mode (`STOREFRONT_SOURCE=fixture`) stays available locally for the evidence lanes. The source is read by one rule (`storefront/src/source/mode.ts`): an explicit `fixture` or `live`; absent or empty selects fixture **only outside a provider context**; in a Vercel build or runtime (`VERCEL` / `VERCEL_ENV` set) an absent, empty, whitespace-only or misspelled value **fails closed** at build time and at request time with the misconfiguration error, so a public deployment can never silently serve the demo tenant (independent review finding A, corrected 2026-09-24). The immutable HOST-001 demo deployment is historical evidence; before any future rebuild of that demo project a **separately authorized provider action** would have to set `STOREFRONT_SOURCE=fixture` on it.
  2. **The read contract.** Exactly one anon-callable function, `public.storefront_menu(p_slug text) returns jsonb` — `STABLE`, `SECURITY DEFINER` (D-037 as amended), owner `postgres`, `set search_path = ''`, grants `revoke all from public, anon, authenticated; grant execute to anon` (`service_role` keeps the stamped EXECUTE). The slug is the only input (request grammar `^[a-z0-9](?:[a-z0-9-]{0,61}[a-z0-9])?$`); no cookie, session, claim, request setting or tenant key is read. The output is the exact key set in [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.42; **uniform** `{ok:false, error:'not_found', entity:'storefront_menu'}` for invalid, unknown, unpublished, suspended, deleted, timezone-less, non-ILS and inclusive-tax tenants (anti-oracle); typed `payload_limit` at 100 categories / 500 items / 2,000 modifiers / 8,000 options; internals never served (`sku`, kitchen fields, attributes, sizes/variants, storage keys, any org/restaurant/branch/device UUID, staff or membership data). Money is integer minor units and tax is basis points (D-007).
  3. **Schema (D4, D3).** `public.restaurant_storefront_profiles` (1:1 with `restaurants`; composite same-org FKs; the public **slug lives on the profile row** — storage grammar `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, 3–48 characters, reserved words, partial-unique among live rows — immutable in this slice: no history, no 308) and `public.storefront_media` (published WebP derivatives under opaque `{prefix32}/{sha256}.webp` keys) with the **public** bucket `storefront-media` (512 KiB, `image/webp`; writes gated by `app.can_write_storefront_media`, rank ≥ manager over the restaurant). Both tables are RLS-forced with deny policies and **no** role grants: the only reads are the DEFINER read and the manager+ profile read; the only writer is `app.set_restaurant_storefront_profile` (CAS on `version`, typed `version_conflict` / `invalid` reasons, audit `settings.storefront.updated`, public INVOKER wrapper `authenticated`-only). [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §4.7–§4.8 own the fields.
  4. **Publish preconditions (D7 / D7b).** A profile publishes only with a slug, a storefront branch, a resolvable branch timezone, currency `ILS`, **exclusive** (or disabled) tax, at least one live category with a live item, and at least one opening-hours window; `app.storefront_publish_blockers` returns the ordered blocker list to the editor. Inclusive-tax and non-ILS tenants are `not_found` on the public read until decided (**OPEN QUESTION Q-030**).
  5. **Live mode is browse-only.** `service.ordering_enabled` and `service.delivery_enabled` are served as the literal `false` (DB CHECK `browse_only`); the client treats `'ordering-off'` as the first submit blocker, the send CTA never exists, `/checkout`, `/payment` and `/review` bounce to `/cart`, and `/r/*` (received / status) is the 404 document in live mode. One new string family `orderingOffline{Title,Body}` × ar/he/en (D5). Existing local carts are preserved (the cart key is per slug + `menu_version`).
  6. **Freshness (D6).** A document is up to 60 s stale and is served stale for at most 300 s more while it regenerates; **no** on-demand invalidation in this slice (**OPEN QUESTION Q-027**). A definitive `not_found` renders the 404 document; a transport failure or the 5 s server timeout **throws** (ISR keeps the last good document; a never-cached slug gets the framework error page) — **never** the fixture and never a fabricated menu.
  7. **Media / CSP (D3).** Images are immutable public derivatives (no signing, no per-image round trip); the CSP `img-src` is widened by **exactly** the project's Supabase origin; `image_url` is served only for rows with `published_at is not null and unpublished_at is null`, as a relative storage path the server adapter makes absolute on `STOREFRONT_SUPABASE_URL`. *(STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001, 2026-09-25: immutable on the sanctioned path only — the registration-scoped storage policies let a manager of the restaurant overwrite a registered object, **OPEN QUESTION Q-033**, and the database never reads the bytes, **OPEN QUESTION Q-036**.)*
  8. **Not in this slice** (each is its own future Work ID): `web_order_requests`, real checkout submission, payment, WhatsApp, a POS inbox, a delivery backend beyond the read-presentation fields, analytics, the Dashboard profile editor / publish action, the hosted apply, provider settings (Deployment Protection, env vars — D9), the plan / quota check (D9b), quantity-enabled modifier groups beyond plain multi-select (D10), on-demand invalidation, slug history.
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - *A BFF / edge proxy in front of the database* — rejected: a second origin to secure, cache and monitor; the same-origin server read gives the same key isolation with one moving part.
  - *Grant `anon` USAGE on `app` and keep the INVOKER-wrapper pattern* — rejected: it widens the boundary every existing suite pins (T-016 B1) for the sake of one function.
  - *`GRANT … TO anon, authenticated`* (the Phase 0 draft) — rejected: D-037 point 6; owner preview uses the authenticated reads.
  - *Stay on the static export with per-tenant rebuilds* — rejected: a slug is data, a rebuild per menu change is a build per restaurant per edit, and dynamic slugs cannot be hosted by a static export at all (D13).
  - *`restaurants.slug`* — rejected (D4): the slug is a Storefront concept with its own grammar, reserved words and publish state; it stays on the profile row.
- **Consequences:**
  - (+) The publishable key never appears in a document or client chunk (tested); `anon` still holds no table privilege and no `app` USAGE; a direct caller with the anon key sees exactly what the site shows.
  - (+) One function, one grant line and one pgTAP suite (T-017) per public read; T-016 fails when anything else joins the allowlist.
  - (−) A shared-engine fan-out: the storefront config-hash pin changes, so under the reviewed engine behaviour this change builds **all three provider projects once per environment event** — three Preview builds on the first push and three Production builds at the merge (six for READ-001 alone; three more if PR #286 is merged on its own first) — never "3 builds" without that per-event qualifier (recorded in the evidence packs before any push).
  - (−) The measurement lanes are re-baselined on a local `next start` snapshot instead of an `out/` export.
  - (−) SEC-001 (`20260913164029`) must **never** be re-applied as a drift remedy once the allowlist is non-empty: its schema-wide `revoke … from anon` would strip the grant and its own assertion would abort ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §16).
  - (−) No DB-side read throttle exists; the bound is the role-level `anon` `statement_timeout` (a hosted read, **OPEN QUESTION Q-028**), the CDN in front of the site and the spend / egress alerts.
- **Related:** D-007, D-008, D-011, D-012, D-013, D-031, D-032, D-037 (as amended), [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.42, [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §4.7–§4.8, [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §3 Layer 3 and §14 T-016 / T-017, [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §2 / §6 / §7, [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §16, [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-025..Q-030, **RISK R-003**, **RISK R-008**.

## D-040 — STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001: storefront media publication via one caller-JWT Edge Function running focused, pinned WASM codecs (Option C2, recipe `storefront-media-c4`)

- **Status:** **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9), implemented **locally** under Work ID **STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001** on the publication branch `feat/STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001-v2-focused-codecs` (created from `main` `6cf6c20f`), from the sealed plan (`worktrees/output/storefront-publish-001-plan-20260924T085908Z/`, outside git) as amended by the sealed Option C spike (`worktrees/output/storefront-publish-001-media-c-spike-20260924T164159Z/`) and two owner packets of 2026-09-25: "OWNER APPROVED — STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001 OPTION C FULL LOCAL IMPLEMENTATION" (Option C, the hosted CPU accounting and the reliability amendment recorded below) and "OWNER APPROVED — STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001 Q031 REMEDIATION + CORRECTION PASS" (the engine replacement of point 1 and the corrections that close the independent review `worktrees/output/storefront-publish-001-independent-review-20260925T105015Z/`, outside git). The sealed plan's decisions D2–D5 and D7–D9 stand; its D1 (media pipeline B1) and D6 (the `dart test packages/storefront_media` CI step that belonged to B1) are superseded by this decision. **Not yet ratified:** pending independent review of the v2 branch and human approval at the merge gate. **Nothing is pushed or hosted:** `supabase/migrations/20260925100000_storefront_publish_001.sql` is local-only, the Edge Function `storefront-media-publish` is **not deployed**, no secret, provider setting, build or deployment changes, and publishing the v2 branch is itself an owner decision ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.1 step 0). *(STOREFRONT-INTEGRATE-001, 2026-10-12: since this status was written, migration `20260925100000` was applied to hosted (2026-09-25) and the branch is integrated into `main` **dark** — the Dashboard editor compiled off, the function not deployed, nothing published ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17). The owner's approval of that integration is **not** by itself the ratification of D-040..D-042: they stay **PROPOSED**, and ratifying them remains the owner's decision; the next hosted step is the bounded hosted pilot of the owner decision of 2026-10-12.)* *(STOREFRONT-PRODUCTION-ENABLE-001, 2026-10-12: the pilot passed (owner-accepted) and the Storefront is production-enabled — the function deployed permanently, the Dashboard editor built open only in Production ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17); no real restaurant is published. D-040..D-042 stay **PROPOSED**: ratifying them remains the owner's decision.)*
- **Context:** READ-001 (**D-039**) created `storefront_media`, the public bucket `storefront-media` with its registration-keyed storage policies, and a public read that serves `logo_url` / `hero_url` only for a LIVE derivative the profile points at — but no write path. Publishing needs a deterministic, content-addressed WebP derivative (≤ 512 KiB) of an **existing private original** (plan decisions D3 / D4), produced without any privileged key. The sealed plan chose **B1** (`package:image` in the Dashboard) behind a spike gate, and the D1 spike **STOPPED** it: the `image` 4.10.1 alpha path produced different bytes on the Dart VM and under dart2js (`worktrees/output/storefront-publish-001-impl-spike-20260924T102512Z/`), so one source would not have had one canonical derivative. The Option C spike then proved a server-side WASM recipe byte-identical between Node and the Supabase edge runtime, and its round-3 verification found a BLOCKER: `RECIPE.variants[variant]` accepted inherited names such as `constructor`. The first implementation (recipe c3, below) vendored an ImageMagick WASM build; the independent review found that binary statically links LGPL components while the repository is **public** (**OPEN QUESTION Q-031**), and the Q031 owner packet replaced the engine.
- **Superseded design (recipe `storefront-media-c3`, evidence only).** The first implementation, on `feat/STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001-profile-editor-media` (head `3e0c3ccb`), ran the same workflow on the vendored `@imagemagick/magick-wasm` 0.0.43 with a 2 MiP decode cap. That wasm statically links LGPL components (glib, libraw, liblqr, libheif, libde265) although the recipe never reached them, and the independent review rated a first push of it to the public repository a BLOCKER (LIC-1). That branch is kept as evidence only: it was **never pushed and must never be pushed**. The v2 publication branch was created from `main` and has **never contained** the ImageMagick wasm (no path of it appears anywhere in its history, and its licence test L6 fails if any path or file under the function names the previous engine).
- **Decision:**
  1. **One server-side deriver on focused codecs (Option C2, recipe `storefront-media-c4`).** A single Supabase Edge Function, `supabase/functions/storefront-media-publish/`, derives the canonical public WebP with recipe **`storefront-media-c4`** on five focused jSquash WebAssembly codecs, vendored byte-exact and unmodified under `vendor/jsquash/` (ten files from four npm packages): `@jsquash/png` **3.1.1** (the PNG decoder; Rust `png` crate), `@jsquash/jpeg` **1.6.0** (the mozjpeg 3.3.1 **decoder** only), `@jsquash/webp` **1.5.0** (the libwebp 1.1.0 **non-SIMD** encoder, and the decoder) and `@jsquash/resize` **2.1.1** (Rust `resize` crate). `lib/recipe.mjs` pins the sha-256 of every vendored file, and `createDeriver()` refuses to start unless each of the five `.wasm` files matches its pin, before anything is compiled; there is no `npm:` / `jsr:` / remote import and no floating semver (static guard S3); the function's `.gitattributes` marks `vendor/**` `-text` and the `.wasm` files `binary`, so a `core.autocrlf` checkout never rewrites the pinned bytes. **Why the replacement:** the ImageMagick wasm of recipe c3 statically linked LGPL components and the repository is public (**OPEN QUESTION Q-031**); the licence inventory of the ten actual vendored files (binary evidence cross-checked against the build definitions at each package's gitHead) found **no copyleft component** — no GPL, LGPL, MPL, EPL, CDDL, AGPL, OSL or EUPL code is linked (`vendor/README.md`). **Upgrade rule:** any byte change to a vendored file is a new recipe id with new goldens, a re-run licence inventory, a regenerated notices file and a new owner decision (`vendor/README.md`). The Dashboard never encodes WebP, and no browser- or Android-side canonical hash exists.
  2. **Caller JWT only.** The function reads exactly two environment settings, `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, and sends every GoTrue, PostgREST and Storage request with the **caller's** bearer JWT plus the anon key (plain `fetch`, zero dependencies); it never reads or uses a `service_role`, secret or storage (S3) key — the platform-default `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS` / `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` may be present in the hosted function environment, and their presence is expected; no custom admin or S3 secret is added for it. Only a signed-in, **non-anonymous** principal whose role and audience are `authenticated` may publish: an anonymous session (a paired device or a kiosk) or any other role or audience is refused `401 unauthenticated` after GoTrue answers and before any RPC or download (review SEC-1). Gateway JWT verification stays on (`supabase/config.toml` `[functions.storefront-media-publish]` `verify_jwt = true`) in addition to the function's own GoTrue check on every request. *(STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26, **DECISION D-041** point 2: the gateway check is defence in depth, not the authentication boundary — by the platform design documented at the time (Supabase's pages disagree with each other) it lets through the project's publishable and secret API keys on either header, and it was observed to let a signed-out session's token through, so the function's own check, which refuses them before any upstream call or after GoTrue answers, is the boundary; the hosted canary re-observes both layers on every run ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 4), and turning the check off later must re-run that item with every probe required to reach the function.)* The requirement is owned by [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §3 Layer 3.
  3. **Resource contract c4 (one call, bounded before decode).** The whole container is inspected before any decoder runs: PNG, 8-bit JPEG (baseline, extended sequential or progressive, with 1 or 3 components — a 4-component CMYK / YCCK JPEG is refused as `unsupported_format`) and still WebP, recognised by magic bytes only; animation is refused. Every decode raster is at most **8,388,608 px (8 MiP)** and **8,192 px per side**, with an aspect ratio of at most **8:1**, checked before decode; the c3 metadata caps (≤ 1 MiB per PNG / WebP metadata chunk and of total JPEG APPn + COM payload, PNG chunk counts) and the JPEG scan caps (at most 16 scans; for a progressive JPEG, scans × coefficients ≤ 400,000,000) are kept. **Two decoder memory budgets** (correction pass, review FN-V5) are checked from the headers before any inflate or decode, and both refuse as `too_many_pixels`: the PNG image data the IHDR implies (filter bytes and Adam7 included) must be ≤ **33,570,816 bytes** (32 MiB + 16 KiB) — every 8-bit RGBA PNG under the 8 MiP cap fits, plain or Adam7, but a 16-bit RGBA PNG only up to about 4 MiP (2048 × 2048) — and a progressive JPEG's DCT coefficients (2 bytes each) must total ≤ **32 MiB** (33,554,432 bytes; recipe c3 allowed 80 MiB) — every 4:2:0 progressive JPEG under 8 MiP fits, but a 4:4:4 one (no chroma subsampling) only up to about 5.3 MiP (for example 2728 × 2048). **PNG:** only `IHDR`, `PLTE`, `tRNS`, `IDAT` and `IEND` reach the decoder (every ancillary chunk is stripped), and the image data is inflated once, fed in 4 KiB steps, bounded by the exact filtered-scanline size the IHDR implies (Adam7-aware), with the zlib header and Adler-32 trailer checked — a decompression bomb is refused as `corrupt` before the decoder runs (review EDGE-1). **JPEG:** any libjpeg decoder warning refuses the source (`decode_warning`), and the decoded size must equal the header's. EXIF orientation is applied for **JPEG only** (integer pixel copies after the resize). **Geometry:** an exact integer k × k premultiplied box pre-reduction when k ≥ 2, then a triangle resize (premultiplied alpha, sRGB values) into the W × W box (`w480` / `w960`), never upscaled or cropped. **Encode:** one lossy WebP encode per call (method 2, `exact`, alpha kept **lossless** at `alpha_quality` 100, never flattened) on the quality ladder 82 / 74 / 66 / 58 / 50 — one rung per call (`202 ladder_next`) — within 524,288 bytes or a typed `output_too_large`; the output self-check requires exactly `VP8X,ALPH,VP8` when any alpha value is below 255 and `VP8` otherwise, with no ICCP / EXIF / XMP chunk and no animation, so **metadata is never copied**; width, height, bytes and sha-256 are read from the actual output. **Instances:** the Emscripten codecs (the JPEG decoder, the WebP decoder and encoder) get a fresh instance per call; the PNG decoder and the resize module are one instance per worker, and any exception out of them poisons the engine — the request keeps its typed refusal and the worker then retires (review FN-V1). *(Amended by **DECISION D-041** point 3 (STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26): the PNG decoder and the resize module also get a **fresh instance per call** — a compiled module may stay cached per worker, while every call instantiates it afresh from the pinned glue's own text wrapped as a per-call factory — implemented and reviewed under Work ID STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001 before any canary run; the vendored bytes and their pins are unchanged, so the upgrade rule of point 1 is not triggered, and the poisoning and retirement rule above is kept unchanged. Reason: in one local worker held busy by a pending request (edge runtime `v1.74.3`), one PNG decoder and resize module per worker hit the 256 MiB memory limit after 6–11 derives (10 kills in 100 derives; their heaps reached up to 91 + 70 MiB depending on call order, with uncollected per-call codec memory on top). **Superseded planning measurements:** those figures, and the planning claim that fresh instances derived 520 requests in one worker without a kill, also at a 192 MiB limit, came before the sealed run and are replaced by it. **Sealed result** ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 3; run locally on the shipped tree of the final candidate `b4845d6d`, which contains STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001 and STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001B, on edge runtime `v1.74.3` and `v1.77.1`, each by recorded digest): the `f0b1002f` negative control hits the memory limit within its first 100 derives on both releases; the candidate passes 520 derives / 30 rounds / one worker / 0 kills at 256 MiB, 166 derives / 10 rounds / one worker / 0 kills at 192 MiB, and 30/30 simultaneous pairs / 0 kills at 256 MiB, on both releases, with every output byte-identical to its golden.)* *(STOREFRONT-MEDIA-ENGINE-FAILURE-001, 2026-09-27, **PROPOSED DECISION D-042**: of the exceptions out of the PNG decoder or the resize module, only a non-trap one thrown by the decode or resize operation keeps the typed refusal (a trap stays `503`); a failure to create or initialise the call's fresh instance never reads the source and answers `503 engine_unavailable` with `retryable:true`; in both cases the engine is poisoned and the worker retires. Implemented under the same Work ID (Phase B, commit `883d7aec`).)* **Init self-test (review EDGE-4):** before its first use the engine derives an embedded 1000 × 400 palette PNG with `tRNS` at `w480` and compares the sha-256 with its pinned golden; a mismatch (for example a platform runtime change that alters the codecs' output) fails closed as `503 engine_unavailable` instead of minting new hashes. Private bytes are never logged or returned. **Why 8 MiP:** the cap was set by measurement on the local edge runtime (v1.74.3) in the Q031 spike — at 12 MiP the worker hit its memory limit (with one codec instance per worker) or reached about 237 MiB of external memory (with fresh instances), while at 8 MiP there were no kills, at most 185 MiB of external memory and a worst derive of 360 ms (figures reported by the spike; no raw log of them was retained). During the correction pass a worker was reported cancelled by its supervisor after a 16-bit RGBA PNG at 8 MiP followed by a 4:4:4 progressive JPEG (recorded in the `4a37e612` commit message; no raw log retained) — the per-worker PNG decoder's heap never shrinks, and a progressive decode holds its whole coefficient buffer next to the raster — which is why the two budgets above were added. **What the local stress proves, and what it does not:** the final stress of the committed recipe at the v2 head on the edge runtime (twelve accepted worst-case sources — 16-bit RGBA PNG at 2048 × 2048 and 8-bit RGBA PNG at 3456 × 2427, each plain and Adam7; 8-bit RGB PNG, baseline, progressive 4:2:0 and progressive grayscale JPEG, lossless, lossy and lossy-alpha WebP, each at 3456 × 2427 (8,387,712 px); progressive 4:4:4 JPEG at 2728 × 2048) derived all 120 requests of five rounds of w960 + w480 (at most 195 MiB of external memory, worst derive 373 ms) and all 12 sources in a fresh 256 MB worker each under the hosted-like 1 s soft / 2 s hard CPU limits (worst derive 341 ms, worst round trip 956 ms). The 120 requests were **not** served by one reused worker: the runtime ran them in at least 54 worker instances of at most 6 derives each and logged 64 early terminations, so **long-lived single-worker memory accumulation was not proven locally** (the earlier claim "one reused 256 MB worker, 120 derives, 0 kills" is withdrawn). That residual is a **STOP item of the hosted canary** ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 3): if the platform does not keep one worker long enough to assess accumulation, it is recorded as UNPROVEN and production publication stops. *(STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26, **DECISION D-041** point 3: the platform cannot be made to keep one worker, so the residual is now closed **locally**, where reuse can be forced — one worker held busy for at least 500 derives at the 256 MiB limit, a run at 192 MiB, two-way co-residency and a negative control against the per-worker design, on two edge-runtime releases — under stated **ASSUMPTIONS** about the hosted runtime (**OPEN QUESTION Q-041**); the hosted canary requires that no worker it ran was killed for memory, and records each worker's shutdown sample as a post-request point sample, not a peak. A missing or failed local proof, or a hosted Memory kill, still stops production publication.)* **Consequence:** a 12 MP phone photo (4000 × 3000 = 12,000,000 px) is refused `too_many_pixels`, as are a 16-bit RGBA PNG above about 4 MiP and a 4:4:4 progressive JPEG above about 5.3 MiP. The Branding card accepts receipt logos up to 4,096 px per side and 16,000,000 px, so a receipt logo above 8 MiP (for example 3000 × 3000), or a 16-bit PNG or 4:4:4 progressive JPEG above its budget, passes Branding but cannot become the storefront logo; the logo slot then names the remedy, with both limits (point 10). Widening this envelope is a new owner decision.
  4. **Exact enumerations.** The variant is exactly `w480` | `w960`, the source bucket exactly `menu-images` | `restaurant-logos` and the slot exactly `logo` | `hero`, checked by `Map` membership of primitive strings or an exhaustive `switch` — never by indexing an ordinary object with untrusted text — with regression tests over every `Object` / `Function` / `Array` / `String` prototype name (the Option C spike's round-3 BLOCKER). A request body that names a top-level key twice is refused (`400 invalid_request`, reason `duplicate_key`) instead of letting `JSON.parse` keep the last value, and the storage-key encoder refuses empty, `.` and `..` segments (review SEC-4). The SQL side checks the bucket and variant vocabularies with `IN` lists (slots exist only in the function).
  5. **Hosted CPU accounting (owner-resolved).** Supabase documents the 2 s CPU limit **per request** (async I/O excluded); the local CLI edge runtime meters CPU per worker isolate across requests (soft 1 s retire / hard 2 s kill), a local-runtime difference. The c4 derive time measured on the local edge runtime stays in the hundreds of milliseconds per call (the slowest vector of the final three-runtime parity run 283 ms; the final stress of the accepted worst-case layouts, worst derive 373 ms across at least 54 short-lived worker instances and 341 ms in a fresh worker per source), so the spike's in-function CPU guard is **not** carried into the implementation. A **hosted canary / performance gate is REQUIRED** before any publication or deploy ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4); it is not a prerequisite of local work. *(STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26 — **DECISION D-041** point 1: the per-request limit above stands. The hosted runtime reports CPU only as the `cpu_time_used` of each worker's terminal event — per the edge-runtime source a total over everything the worker ran, its boot and engine self-test included (the hosted accounting is **OPEN QUESTION Q-039**) — and a worker can serve several requests, so the canary sends each worst-case vector exactly once and bounds it by the total of a worker that served that request alone, a conservative upper bound, at 75 % of the 2 s limit, and never uses the total of a worker that served several requests as a per-request figure ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 2). Whether the hosted runtime also retires a worker at a CPU threshold is part of that question; it does not block the gate. The in-function CPU guard stays out: a user worker cannot read its CPU (`process.cpuUsage()` returns zeros), and the guard's only remedies — refusing work or ending its own isolate — add refusals or end co-resident requests. If the gate fails, the remedy is a narrower c4 envelope — a new owner decision (point 3).)*
  6. **Reliability model (owner amendment): recovery, not typed kills.** A hard termination need not surface as a typed error. Required instead, and implemented: a bounded, validated input envelope; stage → upload → finalize idempotent and durable under keys derived from ONE caller `request_id`; content-addressed objects that are read first and compared, never overwritten (a retry never re-sends bytes that are already there); nothing public **references** a derivative until `finalize` has checked its object and a profile slot points at the LIVE row, so no profile can point at an unfinalized object (an uploaded object is fetchable by anyone holding its URL from the moment of upload — the bucket is public and its name is a content address, not a secret; review STG-3); authoritative reconciliation after any unknown outcome (the Dashboard re-reads `list_storefront_media` and the profile); safe recovery from a STAGED row (retry the same request, or cancel the row); a retry after a killed request is safe. Contract: [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44.
  7. **The database write path.** Five RPCs — `stage_storefront_media`, `finalize_storefront_media`, `cancel_storefront_media`, `retract_storefront_media`, `list_storefront_media` ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.43) — as `SECURITY INVOKER` public wrappers over `app.*` `SECURITY DEFINER` bodies, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and `anon` and **granted to `authenticated` on both layers** (the sealed DB_SECURITY_DELTA §3 revoked the `app` bodies from `authenticated`, which would have made every wrapper `42501` for everyone — corrected here); manager+ authority, with the restaurant proven to belong to the organization before any authority branch or input validation (a foreign or unknown restaurant raises `42501` and leaves no audit row under a foreign id); idempotency per (actor, request id), with every replay of `stage`, `finalize` and `retract` re-reading its row (a replayed `stage` answers `stale_request` once the row was cancelled, a replayed `finalize` once the row was retracted or deleted, and a replayed `retract` once the row was deleted or is LIVE again — review DB-4); `finalize` checking on every call — a replay and an already-LIVE row included — that an object **row** exists under the registered key whose **declared** metadata says `image/webp` and exactly the staged size (it never reads the bytes: review DB-1, **OPEN QUESTION Q-036**); lock order restaurant → profile → media; append-only audit (`settings.storefront.media.{staged, published, cancelled, retracted, denied}`, and `settings.storefront.updated` with reason `media_replaced` for a re-point). The live-source unique index becomes LIVE-only and the state model stays derived from the two timestamps ([STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §13, [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §4.8). The `anon` allowlist and the public `SECURITY DEFINER` set stay exactly `storefront_menu(text)` (asserted by the migration's final block). The READ-001 routines and the four storage policies are **unchanged**.
  8. **Public bucket semantics.** `storefront-media` stays **public**. The **sanctioned path** — the function — uploads only transformed derivatives, never a private original; the database proves only declared metadata, so a manager+ who calls the RPCs and the Storage API directly can publish non-canonical bytes on their own restaurant's storefront (reviews SEC-3 / DB-2 / STG-1; **OPEN QUESTION Q-036**). STAGED means "not referenced by the Storefront public data contract", not "private". No path deletes a `storefront-media` object (**OPEN QUESTION Q-032**).
  9. **Scope (plan decisions D2 / D3 / D4).** Logo = `w480` from the restaurant's current receipt logo (`restaurant-logos`); hero = `w960` from the receipt logo or a menu-item original (`menu-images`). Item images stay **out**: the function has no such slot and `stage_storefront_media` refuses `(menu-images, w480)` — the pair the public read joins for `image_url` — as `variant_not_allowed`; per-item derivatives are a later Work ID (PUBLISH-002 in the sealed plan).
  10. **Dashboard (plan decisions D5 / D7 / D8 / D9).** The Settings Storefront card built under this Work ID exposes `locale_default` and `pause_reason` with truthful help text although the public storefront does not use them today (D5); it is gated by the server verdict of the profile read, not by the client-side role (D7); it shows the public address `/s/<slug>` as text, not as a link (D8); and any manager (rank ≥ 2) may publish a profile, bounded by the protected production storefront, with no public exposure until a separate owner decision (D9). Nothing on the card is editable until the profile read answers, and a `not_found` read shows a note and no fields. The slug is chosen once, at creation, behind a confirmation that it is permanent, and is read-only afterwards. The server's publish blockers are listed with where to fix each; publish and unpublish are explicit, confirmed actions, and the published state shown is only ever the server's. Every write carries one request id and an unknown outcome is retried as that same request (a media slot assignment included: same id, same expected version, same patch); a write whose follow-up re-read fails locks editing until a Reload succeeds. There is no ordering or delivery control. Logo sources are the current receipt logo only, re-checked when Publish is pressed; hero sources are the receipt logo or a menu-item original. A deterministic refusal of a write or of a source shows its reason (a request fault — a `400` or a `422` fault code — shows a generic "could not be processed; contact support"); none offers "try again". **Correction pass:** the card keeps its draft and a pending same-request **Try again** when it scrolls out of the lazy Settings list, and a membership, organization, restaurant or role change starts a fresh editor (review DASH-1); overlapping or duplicate weekly windows get an advisory warning, **touching** weekly windows (one closes at the minute the next opens, also across midnight) are an error in the weekly editor that disables Save and Publish until the manager merges them into one continuous period — the editor never merges them itself; touching boundaries created through date exceptions, and touching periods persisted by Unpublish and save, are accepted residuals (**OPEN QUESTION Q-038**, which also owns the server side) — and **Add hours** never adds an exact duplicate (DASH-2 / OQ-2); saved hours the editor cannot read are kept exactly as stored, lock the hours editor until an explicit repair, and add the client-side blocker `hours_unreadable`, so Publish stays disabled (OQ-1); the status line never says "public", and a suspended branch is marked in the branch picker (OQ-3 / DASH-3 / DASH-5; **OPEN QUESTION Q-035**); a receipt logo the function refuses keeps the logo slot's Publish disabled for that key and names the remedy — replace the receipt logo, which also changes printed receipts (CRIT-2). The pause end is entered in the managing device's local time zone: the picker interprets the entered wall time in that zone, labels it with the UTC offset that the device's time-zone rules give for the chosen pause instant (the device's current offset while no pause end is chosen), and stores the resulting absolute `timestamptz` instant (the stored value is an instant, not a device-local time) — an owner-accepted v1 deviation from the sealed plan's branch-zone entry (owner decision, 2026-09-25: no time-zone database dependency and no migration change in this ticket); a branch-time-zone-aware pause editor is **OPEN QUESTION Q-037** (DASH-4; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §4.7). The client rules are owned by [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44 (*Dashboard client*). *(STOREFRONT-INTEGRATE-001, 2026-10-12: integrated into `main` **dark** — the Settings Storefront card is compiled with the editor **off** (`RESTOFLOW_STOREFRONT_EDITOR`, default false, set by no production build; [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17), so a shipped Dashboard can neither publish a profile nor invoke the media function until a later, owner-approved enablement after the bounded hosted pilot. The server surface is unchanged.)*
  11. **Compliance package.** `vendor/README.md` records the provenance of every vendored file (npm `package@version`, `dist.integrity`, tarball sha-1, jSquash gitHead, path inside the tarball, sha-256 and "unmodified"; the upstream versions and commits — libwebp 1.1.0 `d2e245ea`, mozjpeg 3.3.1 `f154ccc0`, the Emscripten and Rust toolchains and crate versions; the IJG statement "This software is based in part on the work of the Independent JPEG Group."; and what is deliberately not vendored). `vendor/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (103,282 bytes, sha-256 `aca02b5e…`) is the final notices file of the licence inventory, shipped byte-exact and never edited by hand; it ships **inside every deploy bundle** as a `static_files` entry of `supabase/config.toml`, next to the five `.wasm` files. `test/license.test.mjs` (in CI) pins the sha-256 of all eleven vendored files, the exact vendor file set, the `static_files` list and the `.gitattributes` rules. This is **an engineering compliance package, not a declaration of legal sufficiency**; publication remains the owner's decision (**OPEN QUESTION Q-031**).
  12. **CI gating of the database proofs (review CRIT-1).** The pgTAP suites this change relies on (T-016, T-017, T-018, the `zz_` concurrency file, RF-019) run only in the `db-tests` CI job, which is **advisory**: per the independent review's read of branch protection, only the `validate` job is a required status check. Branch protection is an owner / provider setting and is not changed here; a green `db-tests` run (or the local CI replica) is a written pre-merge checklist item for this change ([TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6).
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - *B1 — `package:image` in the Dashboard (the sealed plan's choice)* — stopped by its own spike gate: the `image` 4.10.1 alpha path produced different bytes on the Dart VM and under dart2js, so the Dashboard's web and Android builds would have derived different "canonical" bytes from one source.
  - *Keep the ImageMagick engine (recipe c3) and meet its LGPL obligations* (the review's path A: a compliance package with the LGPL source or a written offer and relinking material) — not chosen: the Q031 owner packet replaced the engine instead.
  - *Rebuild a three-coder ImageMagick wasm, or de-vendor it behind a fetch-and-verify pin* (the review's path C) — not chosen by the same packet; the focused codecs are vendored from published npm tarballs, so no custom engine build is needed.
  - *Supabase Image Transformations* — rejected: Pro plan required, WebP not selectable as the output format, byte determinism undocumented, and the path is not testable locally or in CI (sealed plan `MEDIA_PIPELINE_DECISION.md` §2).
  - *Flattening transparency* — rejected by the owner: a logo would lose its alpha channel; the recipe keeps alpha and its tests prove it.
  - *Client-side encoding (browser canvas, `flutter_image_compress`, a WASM encoder inside the Dashboard)* — rejected: bytes depend on the engine and platform (canvas falls back to PNG where WebP encoding is unsupported), and per-platform canonical hashes were excluded by the owner.
  - *A `service_role` function* — rejected (**D-011**): a privileged key would bypass RLS and the RPC authority checks; acting as the caller keeps every existing check authoritative.
  - *`npm:` / `jsr:` imports of the codec packages* — rejected: they would be resolved from a registry at bundle time instead of being committed and pinned byte-for-byte.
  - *A test-only kill or fault hook in the function* — rejected (STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26; **DECISION D-041** point 5): a switch the deployed bundle can read — a request field, a header, the URL or an environment value — is either reachable by any caller (a deliberate kill primitive against the isolate) or depends on a secret that the single hosted project would share with production; static guard S2 allows exactly two environment reads, and static guard S8 — added under STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001 before any canary run — allows three request headers, no URL or query read and no wrapper around the handler; both are lexical guards, with code review as the backstop; a canary-only bundle or function would prove a different artefact from the one deployed; a database fault function adds production schema. A death leaves only the upstream calls that had committed, so a row lock on the synthetic profile, a client abort and the existing engine retirement reach every state a kill can leave on the hosted project, and local true kills cover the rest ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 9).
  - *Keeping the spike's in-function CPU guard* — not carried: hosted meters CPU per request (owner-resolved), so the guard addressed a local-runtime artefact, and the spike itself recorded the guard's safety as unproven.
- **Consequences:**
  - (+) One canonical derivative per source bytes whatever the client platform: the c4 goldens and refusals (`test/goldens.mjs`) are identical on Node 22, Node 24 and edge-runtime v1.74.3 ([TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6).
  - (+) No copyleft component in the shipped codecs, per the licence inventory of the vendored files (an engineering inventory, not legal advice), and the notices travel inside every deploy bundle (point 11). The five `.wasm` files total 801,324 bytes.
  - (+) No privileged key anywhere: RLS, the storage policies and the RPC authority checks remain the only authority; the function cannot delete or overwrite an object.
  - (+) A killed or timed-out request is recovered by retrying the same `request_id`: the Node test double covers kills before and during stage, upload and finalize; the evidence branch's local end-to-end run (recipe c3, the same workflow) converged after a CPU-limit kill and three edge-runtime container kills inside `stage` / `finalize`; the v2 end-to-end result is recorded in [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6. *(STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, 2026-09-26, **DECISION D-041** point 5: on the hosted canary the same states are reached without any test hook — a row lock on the synthetic profile interrupts a publish between upload and `finalize`, a client abort loses an answer mid-flight, and one malformed-PNG publish, never retried, ends a real worker; a death during an upload is proven locally, [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 9.)*
  - (−) A new deployable: CLI (Docker) bundling with the five `.wasm` files and the notices as static files ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.3). The edge-runtime lane is not in CI (the `validate` job is Docker-free): the Node suite runs in CI, while the local end-to-end run and the hosted canary are publication prerequisites; the gateway `verify_jwt` layer is exercised only by the canary.
  - (−) The 8 MiP envelope refuses 12 MP phone photos and receipt logos above 8 MiP (point 3); an owner whose receipt logo is too large must replace it, which also changes printed receipts.
  - (−) Any exception out of the PNG decoder or the resize module retires the worker: a manager can trigger that on purpose with a malformed PNG, and other requests in flight on that isolate — possibly of other callers — may then end without a contract body. Those callers see an unknown outcome and retry with the same `request_id`, which converges (reviews EDGE-2, FN-V1).
  - (−) *(STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001, **DECISION D-041**)* The requests in flight on one worker share its memory: three or more simultaneous worst-case derives may reach the memory limit (two keep less than 25 % margin; **OPEN QUESTION Q-040**), and, if the hosted runtime accumulates CPU per worker (**OPEN QUESTION Q-039**), requests admitted to one worker could together reach a CPU limit; the worker is then terminated with every request in flight on it, possibly of other callers. Those callers see an unknown outcome and retry with the same `request_id`, which converges (point 6). A client cannot force or prevent co-residence; the canary fails on any CPU or memory kill it observes ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 items 2 and 3).
  - (−) A documented runtime residual of the PNG image-data bound: on Node 22 only, a zlib stream followed by extra bytes whose last four bytes equal the Adler-32 of the image data passes the bound and the PNG decoder decides; Node 24 (the CI runtime) and the edge runtime (production) refuse it (recipe test R11; review FN-V2).
  - (−) Egress: every invocation downloads the private original again, and the Dashboard sends at most 10 invocations per publish press (5 rungs, each at most twice), so one press can read up to 10 × the original's bucket cap — 50 MiB for a `menu-images` hero (5 MiB cap), 20 MiB for a receipt logo (2 MiB cap) — plus the ≤ 512 KiB content-address reads and one upload; each **Try again** can repeat it, and there is no per-caller rate limit (review CRIT-4; [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.2 records the plan's egress quota).
  - (−) The canonical recipe, like the rung order, is a property of the honest client: the database cannot tell the function from any manager's JWT (**OPEN QUESTION Q-036**).
  - (−) The database proofs are advisory in CI (point 12).
  - (−) Open follow-ups: **Q-031** (answered for the publication branch by replacement; the owner still decides publication, and the evidence branch is never pushed); **Q-032** (removal of retracted, orphaned and unwanted objects; no product path can honour a removal request today); **Q-033** (a state-aware storage gate); **Q-034** (the READ-001 opening-hours validator defect, not fixed here); **Q-035** (publish blockers for a suspended or soft-deleted tenant and for `payload_limit`; the media write path ignores tenant status); **Q-036** (the canonical recipe is enforced by the function, not by the database).
- **Related:** D-007, D-011, D-012, D-013, D-022, D-037 (as amended), D-039, [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.43–§4.44, [STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §13, [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §4.7–§4.8, [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §3 Layer 3 and §14 T-018, [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6, [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17, [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-031..Q-036, Q-039, Q-040, Q-041, D-041 (the canary gate), D-042 (engine-initialisation failures), **RISK R-003**.

## D-041 — STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001: the hosted canary gate of D-040 reconciled with Supabase Edge Runtime semantics

- **Status:** **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9), Work ID **STOREFRONT-CANARY-GATE-001** — the dedicated architecture / contract-change Work ID of this gate — on the D-040 publication branch lineage (`feat/STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001-v2-focused-codecs`, PR #288, unmerged); owner approval 2026-09-26. Its docs step changes no code, configuration, hosted state or data. The code change of point 3 and the static guard S8 are implemented under Work ID **STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001**; the unit test F38 and the Dashboard's platform-failure test that point 2 requires are a separately authorized test-only follow-up under this Work ID; each is reviewed before any canary run, and no Dashboard runtime code changes. *(STOREFRONT-INTEGRATE-001, 2026-10-12: PR #288 is integrated into `main` dark; status unchanged — see the D-040 Status note.)*
- **Context:** The first hosted canary run of [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 (2026-09-26; sealed pack `storefront-publish-001-edge-canary-run-20260926T015005Z`, outside git) ended **NOT PASSED — STOP**: item 1 failed (one `503 upstream_unavailable` after 15.2 s — an owner-supplied calibration read of that run's logs, to be sealed with the next run's pre-registration, attributes it to an upstream (Storage) timeout of the function's 15 s bound before any write, not a CPU or memory failure), item 4 failed (requests carrying only the publishable key reached the function), and items 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 9 and 10 were not proven — several because §17.4 asked for what Supabase does not expose or let a client control. The platform facts this decision rests on (Supabase documentation, the edge-runtime source and that run's own logs): the CPU limit is documented as 2 s **per request**, while a worker stays active for the plan's wall-clock limit and can serve several requests; the only hosted CPU figure is the `cpu_time_used` of each worker's terminal event, a total over the worker's life; memory is enforced on heap + external memory, WebAssembly memory included, a worker at half its limit is retired only once idle, and the only hosted memory figure is one post-request sample per worker at shutdown; a client cannot choose worker reuse or co-location; the gateway's `verify_jwt` check lets the project's publishable and secret API keys through by the design documented at the time (Supabase's pages disagree with each other) and was observed to let a signed-out session's token through; no API kills a chosen worker or request. This decision re-grades nothing: the 2026-09-26 run keeps its verdict.
- **Decision:**
  1. **CPU per request, measured conservatively (keeps D-040 point 5).** The documented 2 s per-request limit stays the contract, with the old margin: every worst-case request at most 75 % of it (1,500 ms). Because no per-request figure exists, each of the 14 named worst vectors is sent exactly once, alone on its worker — the next vector only after the previous worker's terminal event — and the terminal `cpu_time_used` of a worker that served exactly that one request, which per the edge-runtime source also contains the worker's boot and engine self-test, is the request's conservative upper bound; a vector whose worker served another request, or whose terminal event does not appear within the verified tier's documented wall-clock limit + 60 s, is a miss and the run is NOT PASSED — STOP (a vector is never re-sent). The total of a worker that served several requests is never used as a per-request figure. A separate overlap burst, sent only after the fourteenth vector's terminal event, is a resource-pressure check only — never a per-request measurement — and must end without any CPU kill, and no `CPUTime` termination or `546` answer may occur anywhere in the run ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 2). How the hosted telemetry accumulates, and whether a worker is retired at a CPU threshold, is **OPEN QUESTION Q-039** — a question to Supabase that does not block the gate. A failed item 2 is remedied by a narrower c4 envelope — a new owner decision; the ceiling changes only under point 7, derived from documented platform limits, never from calibration or run data, and never for the run that motivated it. The in-function CPU guard stays out (D-040 point 5).
  2. **Gateway as defence in depth.** `verify_jwt = true` stays; the authentication boundary is the function's own check. §17.4 item 4 requires a gateway refusal only where the platform documents one (no credential; a JWT it cannot verify) and, for every other credential that is not a genuine signed-in session — an API key, the legacy anon key, a signed-out, anonymous or expired session — a `401` with zero privileged work at either layer, proven by the answer, a discriminating body and unchanged rows and objects on the hosted project, and at the deployed bytes by the required unit tests F2, F29, F34 and F38 and a local real-GoTrue lane per credential class; the function's own CORS headers are contracted exactly, and every gateway refusal of a Dashboard-shaped request must be readable by the Dashboard ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44). Because Supabase's pages on the check disagree, the gateway is re-observed on every run; a later decision to turn `verify_jwt` off must re-run §17.4 item 4 (b) with every probe required to reach the function.
  3. **Fresh codec instances and a local memory proof (amends D-040 point 3).** The PNG decoder and the resize module get a fresh WebAssembly instance per call, like the other codecs — a compiled module may stay cached per worker, but no instance, and so no codec heap or state, is reused across calls — implemented under Work ID **STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001** with its own tests and review (vendored bytes and pins unchanged; the poisoning and retirement rule kept); the canary runs only on a bundle that contains it. Long-lived-worker accumulation is proven locally, where reuse can be forced — one busy worker, at least 500 derives at 256 MiB, a run at 192 MiB, two-way co-residency and a negative control against the per-worker design, on edge runtime `v1.74.3` and on the latest release at the time of the run — under **ASSUMPTIONS** about the hosted runtime (**OPEN QUESTION Q-041**): that it counts heap + external memory (a malloced-memory term is covered by the 192 MiB run), that its limit is at least 244.1 MiB (covered by the same run) and that it behaves as one of the tested releases. The canary requires zero Memory kills, zero `546` answers and no `exceeded` flag, and records each worker's shutdown sample as a post-request point sample (samples above 192 MiB carry forward as adverse facts). Two simultaneous worst-case derives keep less than 25 % margin and three are unproven (**OPEN QUESTION Q-040**). Reason: in a local busy worker, the per-worker instances of `f0b1002f` hit the 256 MiB limit after 6–11 derives (10 kills in 100 derives), so accepting them would weaken review V-2.
  4. **Logs and worker lifecycles.** §17.4 item 6 is a pre-registered, server-side classification of the run's own logs, through a pinned read-only path whose key names were validated on real rows before sealing: a closed allowlist of the function's two log texts and one runtime delivery-error class on named workers, secret and identifier detectors, exactly one terminal event per worker, `EarlyDrop` and `TerminationRequested` on a worker without a lost request (one whose answer the run plan did not name in advance) accepted as the runtime's normal ends, exactly one induced engine retirement required (never retried), and one sealed per-worker table from which items 2 and 3 are judged; no CPU figure is estimated.
  5. **Kill convergence without a hook.** No fault or kill hook is in, or is added to, the function; static guard S2 and, from STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001 on, static guard S8 (lexical, with code review as the backstop) keep it so. §17.4 item 9 proves convergence from every state a death can leave: local true kills (a death during an upload included), a hosted row-lock interruption between upload and `finalize` observed through the Dashboard's own reconciliation, a hosted mid-flight client abort, one hosted engine retirement never retried, and a whole-run final-state check. That Storage records an object only after a complete upload is inferred for the hosted Storage unless its version is recorded; otherwise a death during an upload is a named residual (**OPEN QUESTION Q-032**).
  6. **One run, one verdict.** Evidence is never combined across canary runs: each run is judged on its own evidence and a rerun repeats every item; every failure, anomaly and deviation of an earlier sealed run is mapped to a row of the later run that re-observes it; the production deploy of §17.1 step 3 carries the `ezbr_sha256` (the CLI's attestation) and the shipped-file hashes of the canary run whose verdict it uses. A rule for combining evidence would be its own Work ID.
  7. **Gate change control and run discipline.** A §17.4 criterion changes only through the architecture-change procedure, before the run it governs; each run's plan — pacing, windows, timeouts, counts, read schedule, log queries, evaluator, upstream health thresholds and the harness's secret-handling invariants — is sealed before its deploy and named in the owner's authorization; a run starts only after an upstream health GO check of the calls the function makes passes, and there is no health check, pause or resume after the first workload request: any unexpected `5xx`, `546`, transport failure or other platform or upstream failure after that point is a miss; STOP means that no new test case or workload is started after the first unexpected miss, and only bounded read-only evidence capture, authoritative re-reads, a same-`request_id` resend of an already-started request with an unknown outcome (which never erases the miss), the authorized synthetic cleanup, the function's removal, the cleanup verification and sealing continue; a run is never repeated merely until one passes — after a failed run, a new run needs a root-cause review and a fresh owner authorization; the plan tier is verified in the PRE, never inferred, and selects the wall-clock limit that item 2 uses (an unverifiable tier stops the run before the deploy); an independent read-only log observer runs the run-wide log queries, and the operator holds no log credential; every browser check runs without request interception, so every preflight is the browser's own; the key check of §17.3 is the deployed function's own first answer to a synthetic session.
- **Assumptions and named residuals** (each restated in, and where noted accepted by, the run's authorization before its deploy): the hosted memory accounting, limit and runtime (point 3; **Q-041**); two-way memory co-residency below 25 % margin with three-way unproven (**Q-040**); requests co-resident on one worker reaching a CPU limit together, if the hosted runtime accumulates CPU per worker (**Q-039**; recovered by D-040 point 6); a death during a hosted upload when the hosted Storage version is not recorded (point 5, owner-accepted; **Q-032**).
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - *Keep §17.4 as written* — rejected: items 2, 3, 4 and 9 asked for a per-request CPU figure under overlapping load, a reused worker, a gateway refusal of API keys and a timed hosted kill, which Supabase does not provide, so the gate could only end UNPROVEN while adding no protection.
  - *Re-grade the 2026-09-26 run under the new text* — rejected: retroactive.
  - *Treat the 2 s limit as per worker: hold every worker's total, however many requests it served, to 1,500 ms, and a warm request to 75 % × 2 s minus an assumed 1,000 ms soft limit, scaled from local measurements* — rejected by the owner: it reinterprets the documented per-request limit as a per-worker one, uses a cumulative multi-request figure as a per-request one, and rests on an assumed soft limit and an assumed scaling.
  - *A per-request ceiling of 75 % × (2 s − 1 s) = 750 ms with a worker ceiling of 1,750 ms* — rejected: it halves the margin and rests on the same per-worker reading.
  - *An in-function CPU guard, or memory self-retirement / admission guards* — rejected: a user worker cannot read its CPU, a wall-time estimate is no bound of the isolate total, and the only remedies refuse legitimate work or kill co-resident requests.
  - *Keep one PNG decoder and resize module per worker and accept the accumulation as a residual* — rejected: a weakening of review V-2 after a local proof that busy workers are killed.
  - *A test-only kill hook, a canary-only bundle or a database fault function* — rejected (point 5; D-040 alternatives).
  - *Drop the hosted row-lock interruption and rely on answers lost after completion* — rejected: it is the only hosted interruption of a request that then converges to LIVE.
  - *Serve the production origins to the browser by request interception, or by a local TLS server behind a host-resolver rule* — rejected: interception answers the browser's preflight itself, so the preflight never reaches the function; the real production origins are loaded instead (their reachability and headers are checked in the §17.2 canary-run PRE), and a local resolver would be considered only if they could not be.
  - *Combine evidence across runs under a reuse ledger* — rejected: in the adopted path nothing would be reusable, and each rule was a further way to void a unit or miss.
  - *Let the owner decide in advance that an attributed, converging upstream transient is not a miss, or allow transient `5xx` answers in general* — rejected: a relaxation of "any unexpected value is STOP"; an upstream health GO check before the workload starts is used instead, and after the start nothing excuses a miss.
  - *Re-send a CPU vector whose worker served another request, or pause and resume the run after an in-run health miss* — rejected by the owner: either would let a run keep trying until a clean observation appears.
  - *Turn `verify_jwt` off, or add an origin allowlist* — out of scope; each would be its own Work ID.
- **Consequences:**
  - (+) Every §17.4 item is observable on Supabase and ends in a binary rule; no earlier requirement is dropped without an equal or stricter replacement, and the old 1,500 ms per-request ceiling is kept.
  - (−) Point 3 changes the deploy bundle; the next canary runs every item again, and so does any later run.
  - (−) Item 2's one-request workers make a run long: each of the 14 vectors waits for its worker's terminal event (at most the verified tier's documented wall-clock limit + 60 s), so a run's live window depends on that limit, and an independent read-only log observer must be available throughout.
  - (−) A demanding canary: an operator-supplied legacy anon key reference, one anonymous sign-in, an expired token minted before the deploy, a capped row lock on one synthetic row, real browsers at both production origins, and a named read-only log observer within the log retention whose queries were validated on real rows.
  - (−) The residuals listed above are recovered by the D-040 point 6 retry.
- **Related:** D-011, D-012, D-013, D-040, [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.2–§17.4, [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44, [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6, [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §3 Layer 3, [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-028, Q-032, Q-039, Q-040, Q-041, **RISK R-002**, **RISK R-003**.

## D-042 — STOREFRONT-MEDIA-ENGINE-FAILURE-001: a failure to create or initialise a fresh PNG or resize codec instance is an engine failure, not a source refusal

- **Status:** **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9), Work ID **STOREFRONT-MEDIA-ENGINE-FAILURE-001** — a dedicated contract-change Work ID — on the D-040 publication branch lineage (`feat/STOREFRONT-PUBLISH-001-v2-focused-codecs` at `42dc70fa`, PR #288, unmerged); owner authorization 2026-09-27. **Phase A** (this entry, [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44, [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6 and [IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST](IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md)) is docs only: no code, test, configuration, vendor, Dashboard runtime, hosted, data or deploy change. **Phase B** — the implementation and its tests — is **implemented locally** on the same lineage (commit `883d7aec`, branch `fix/STOREFRONT-MEDIA-ENGINE-FAILURE-001-engine-init-classification`, not merged): `lib/codecs.mjs` exports the marker `CodecInitError` and each per-call entry wraps exactly its two creation steps in it; `lib/recipe.mjs` maps the marker to `engine_unavailable` and poisons the deriver; the handler is unchanged. Function tests R32–R38 and P2, static guard S8 (the new per-call forms, the mapping and retirement forms, and ten lexical probes), fourteen behavioural mutation probes all killed, the Dashboard tests of point 6, the 74-vector parity check 74/74 on Node 22.14.0, Node 24.21.0 and edge runtime `v1.74.3` / `v1.77.1` ([TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6). The local memory proof of [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 3 has **not** been re-run on this tree; it remains mandatory before any hosted canary. D-042 stays PROPOSED until the #288 lineage is merged. *(STOREFRONT-INTEGRATE-001, 2026-10-12: PR #288 is integrated into `main` dark; status unchanged — see the D-040 Status note.)*
- **Context:** **DECISION D-041** point 3 (implemented under STOREFRONT-MEDIA-MEMORY-001) gives the PNG decoder and the resize module a fresh WebAssembly instance per call, so creating that instance — the per-call factory `freshPng()` / `freshResize()` and `initSync` of the worker's cached compiled module, which constructs the `WebAssembly.Instance` and allocates its memory — now runs inside every request. Those steps never read the source: a failure there (for example an instance whose memory cannot be allocated) says that this worker could not provide a codec, not that the image is invalid. Today `lib/codecs.mjs` runs creation and the operation in one call and `lib/recipe.mjs` classifies a codec exception only by its type, so a non-trap creation failure answers `422 refused` `decode_failed` — a deterministic "pick another source" verdict. On the logo slot the Dashboard remembers such a refusal for the receipt-logo key: it keeps Publish disabled for that key and tells the owner to replace the receipt logo, which also changes printed receipts (D-040 point 10; review CRIT-2) — for an image that may be perfectly valid. Already `503 engine_unavailable` today, and unchanged by this decision: a WebAssembly trap (a `WebAssembly.RuntimeError`) wherever it comes from, and any failure of the engine start, the EDGE-4 self-test derive included.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Creation is not operation.** For the PNG decoder and the resize module, a call has two steps. **Creation / initialisation** is everything up to a ready instance: the per-call factory (`freshPng()` / `freshResize()`) and `initSync` of the cached compiled module. **Operation** is the instance's `decode(…)` / `resize(…)`, from copying the input into the instance's memory to its return. The step that threw decides the class — never the exception's type or message — and an exception that cannot be attributed to a creation step is an operation failure. The rule holds for the resize instance whatever the source format, since PNG, JPEG and WebP sources all use it.
  2. **A creation / initialisation failure answers `503 engine_unavailable` with `retryable:true`.** The body is the existing `{ ok:false, status:'engine_unavailable', retryable:true }` — no `code`, no new field. It is an engine / runtime availability failure: the client retries the same request, which a fresh worker serves (D-040 point 6).
  3. **An operation failure keeps its verdict.** A non-trap exception out of the decode or resize operation keeps `422 refused` `decode_failed` wherever the contract defines it today (review FN-V1); a trap stays `503 engine_unavailable`.
  4. **Poisoning and retirement are unchanged.** Every one of these PNG / resize engine-state failures — creation, operation or trap — poisons the engine and retires the worker (a timer throw after the answer); until it ends, every later request on that worker answers `503 engine_unavailable` without reaching the engine. The poisoning and retirement rule that D-041 point 3 kept stands.
  5. **Everything else is unchanged.** The JPEG decoder and the WebP decoder and encoder keep their behaviour: a fresh instance per call, a non-trap failure — in creating their instance or in the operation — answers `422 decode_failed` without poisoning, and a trap answers `503` and poisons. Also unchanged: recipe `storefront-media-c4`, its goldens and output bytes, the caps, the 8 MiP envelope and the two decoder memory budgets (D-040 point 3), the quality ladder, the memory criteria of D-041 point 3, authentication, CORS, the request shape, the retry rules, the RPCs, RLS and storage. The set of inputs the recipe accepts does not change: the creation steps take nothing from the source, and every derived answer still comes from the unchanged recipe. No new status, code, field or log line is added (a new log line would need its own [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 6 allowlist entry); `engine_unavailable` is only ever the `status` of a `503`, never the `code` of a `422`.
  6. **The Dashboard never records an engine failure as a source refusal.** A `503 engine_unavailable` — an engine start, a trap, or a failure to create or initialise a PNG decoder or resize instance — is never remembered for a receipt-logo key, never disables the logo slot's Publish and never says "pick another source": it takes the unknown-outcome path (one same-request retry on the same rung, then a refresh of the server's state and **Try again** with the same request). Only a contracted `422 refused` is a source refusal, so a genuine operation verdict that the retry receives from a trusted worker stands. The current client already treats every `5xx` this way and reads no refusal from a `503` body, so no Dashboard runtime change is needed; Phase B adds a test that proves it ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44, *Dashboard client*).
  7. **Implementation boundary (Phase B, implemented).** The split lives in `lib/codecs.mjs` (the marker `CodecInitError`; each per-call entry's `try` covers exactly the factory call and `initSync`, and the decode / resize call stays outside it) and `lib/recipe.mjs` (the marker maps to `engine_unavailable` and poisons the deriver, before the trap check): `lib/png_instance.mjs` and `lib/resize_instance.mjs` stay the pinned glue text verbatim (licence test L7), static guard S8 pins the new per-call text — catching an entry whose `try` is removed, covers the operation, or leaves `initSync` outside — and the mapping in `lib/recipe.mjs` and the retiring `engine_unavailable` answer in `lib/handler.mjs`, and the handler's existing mapping of `engine_unavailable` to `503` with retirement carries the new class unchanged. The tests are recorded in [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6.
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - *Keep `422 decode_failed` for every non-trap exception (the status quo)* — rejected: a creation failure is not a verdict on the source, and the logo slot's refusal memory would block the publication of a valid image and push the owner to replace a receipt logo that prints correctly.
  - *Classify by exception type (for example every `RangeError` or `WebAssembly.LinkError` as an engine failure)* — rejected: the type does not identify the step — a `RangeError` can also come from the glue during an operation (copying the output), and a trap can come from either step; the call site does.
  - *Retry the instance creation inside the request* — rejected: it stays in a worker that has just failed to provide a codec and competes with co-resident requests for its memory; a fresh worker, reached through the client's same-request retry (D-040 point 6), is the recovery.
  - *Answer `503` without poisoning or retiring the worker* — rejected: a worker that could not create an instance is not trusted to derive; the conservative poisoning rule of D-040 and D-041 is kept.
  - *A new status or code (for example `instance_unavailable`)* — rejected: it would change the Dashboard's contract (its code lists and the drift test that reads the function's code literals), while `engine_unavailable` already means "retry; a fresh worker serves it".
  - *Apply the same rule to the JPEG decoder and the WebP codecs* — not in this Work ID: the owner scoped it to the fresh PNG and resize instances; their failures do not poison the engine today, and a change there would be its own Work ID.
- **Consequences:**
  - (+) A valid source is no longer refused, and the logo slot no longer blocked, because a worker could not create a codec instance; the same-request retry converges on a fresh worker.
  - (+) No accepted input, output byte, golden, cap or memory criterion changes, and the Dashboard needs no runtime change.
  - (−) The retirement still ends other requests in flight on the isolate without a contract body (review EDGE-2); they retry with the same `request_id`, which converges — unchanged.
  - (−) A creation failure that recurs on every worker (for example a runtime that cannot allocate an instance at all) now shows as a repeated unknown outcome with **Try again**, never as a refusal that names a remedy; the operator sees it through the retirements, not on the card.
  - (−) A named asymmetry: a non-trap failure to create a JPEG or WebP codec instance still answers `422 decode_failed`, without poisoning (point 5).
  - (−) Phase B changed the function's executable files and so its deploy bundle: static guard S8's pinned text changed with it, and the 74-vector parity check was re-established on the Phase B candidate (74/74 on the four runtimes); the local memory proof of [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 item 3 — sealed on the shipped tree of `b4845d6d`, and bound to the exact bundle tree to be deployed — has not yet been re-established on the Phase B tree and must be before any canary, and the canary runs only on a bundle that contains the change.
- **Related:** D-040 (points 3, 6 and 10), D-041 (point 3), [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.44, [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6, [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §17.4 items 3, 6 and 9, [IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST](IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md) (Work IDs), [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-040 (its leaning names the next Work ID that touches the engine for a three- and four-way co-residency measurement).
