The provider's card-hash duplicate variant is not used. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056**: "conclusive" means a `declined` result — an `approved` result keeps the claim until it is recorded or resolved, unless its order was already freed (an `order_freed` orphan); otherwise a new attempt follows only the manager's evidence-bound `free_order` of a LIVE `unknown` / `released` attempt without `charge_may_exist`, or an operator `provider_voided` / `provider_refunded` resolution.)*
  3. **Outcomes.** Approved = the commit reply reports no error, return code zero and an approved approval type, or a lookup reports an approved status (deposited or not). Declined = a declined approval type; any non-zero return code is UNKNOWN until a §4.55 amendment, after TEST-mode discovery (**Q-048**), enumerates decline codes *(pointer amended 2026-10-21 by BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001B; the stricter rule is kept)*. Everything else — timeout, dropped connection, provider error codes, malformed or out-of-contract replies, duplicate refusals — is UNKNOWN. A phone-authorization request is UNKNOWN as well (no phone-authorization flow in the pilot): it is resolved only by the status lookup and the **Q-048** procedure, never by a new attempt. Outcomes reach the POS as typed RETURN envelopes ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.35), never as RAISEs. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 8: a commit approval also requires a tip absent or exactly zero — a non-zero tip is the anomaly `amount_mismatch`, an unreadable one `out_of_contract` — and a commit reply finishes only a still-`sent` row; once a row is `unknown`, `released`, anomalous or resolved, only a status lookup or the operator resolution concludes it.)*
  4. **Money.** Integer minor units everywhere (**D-007**). The provider adapter is the **single permitted decimal boundary**: the request amount is derived from the integer minor amount by integer division and modulo into a canonical two-decimal ASCII lexeme and placed in the outbound JSON as an **unquoted JSON number token** through a tiny validated raw-number serializer (never through a JavaScript `Number`, a double, `parseFloat`, floating-point arithmetic or a locale formatter); no floating-point arithmetic or parsing touches money. Amounts returned by the provider are accepted only as raw token text matching `^(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,8})\.[0-9]{2}$`, converted back to minor units by integer arithmetic and compared with strict equality to the attempt's `amount_minor`, any other shape being the `out_of_contract` provider anomaly *(wording made exact on 2026-10-22, BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001B round 2)*; the currency must be the order currency (ILS for the pilot tenant, the **Q-007** interim) and the provider's currency type must match; any mismatch blocks recording and raises an incident. The PIN-pad tip screen is not used: the tip object is omitted and a non-zero tip amount fails closed (**Q-011** stays DEFERRED). Charge type regular; no installments; no authorization-only or pre-authorization modes. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 9: "arithmetic" means floating-point arithmetic — integer division and modulo of the minor amount are the prescribed derivation; the inbound amount is accepted only as raw token text matching `^(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,8})\.[0-9]{2}$` and anything else is the `out_of_contract` provider anomaly; LIVE amounts are bounded by the operator revision's required `max_live_amount_minor`.)*
  5. **Release, void, refund.** A cashier cancel may send the provider's release call (effective only while the pad still shows its swipe screen); release is advisory — the original attempt is still looked up, and an approval found afterwards is written to the ledger as `approved`, never discarded, and recorded only if its claim is still active. The application makes **no** provider void or refund call in the pilot; **D-023** and **D-024** are unchanged. An `approved_unrecorded` attempt is handled by a manager procedure that re-records under the same identity and never re-charges *(amended 2026-10-21 — resolved by **D-053** point 1: re-record the same identity on the original order first; an approval that can no longer be legally recorded is `orphan_approved` and keeps the order blocked; a same-day provider void is manual, outside the application, only after explicit owner approval; no automatic void, refund or release)*. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 3 and 9: "recorded" means that the matching `payments` row exists — an approval found after a release is written to the ledger as `approved` and recorded only if its claim is still active, otherwise it is an `order_freed` orphan; a provider-reported charge anomaly is a `provider_anomaly` that a manager can never free; an `order_freed` orphan never re-blocks its freed order.)*
  6. **Data minimisation (per response field).** Persist only closed, normalized classes and validated numeric or code fields: the provider reference number, approval number, voucher number, last four digits, card brand code, approval type, the integer return code and an internal normalized return class, entry mode, payment method, status code, deposit id, provider timestamp, and amounts as integer minor units with currency — each only after its field-specific character-set and length rule and the no-card-data rule (no run of 12 or more digits across any punctuation or whitespace, no masked card pattern); a failing field is dropped, never persisted. The vendor's free-text return message is inspected transiently for classification and dropped — never persisted, logged, reported, audited or returned to a client — and no other provider free text is stored *(amended 2026-10-22, BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001B round 3, and 2026-10-23, round 6)*. Never persist, log, print or display: the full or masked card number, BIN, expiry, token, raw or encrypted processor records, the provider's receipt texts, request bodies (they carry the terminal password) and any provider payload outside the allowlist; the server strips these unconditionally, whatever the terminal's own masking setting. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 8: a TEST attempt keeps none of the card or provider-reference fields — no provider reference, approval or voucher number, last four, brand, entry mode, payment method or deposit id; **D-057** point 8(w) lists the fields it keeps.)*
  7. **Audit and isolation.** A new `card_attempt.*` audit action family ships with registry entries, ar/he/en titles and a scalar safe-detail allowlist of state tokens plus the integer minor amount with its currency ([MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §17 item 10; the provider's integer codes and references stay in the ledger) ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.33; wording amended 2026-10-21 by BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001B to align with MONEY §17 item 10); the ledger lives in the `app` schema with forced RLS, explicit deny policies and no app-role grants, is never a `sync_pull` entity (T-003), is read-only for `accountant` (**D-028**) and unreachable from platform support mode. TEST-mode attempts write no tenant Activity-log entry and are retained 90 days unless an incident hold is set (**D-053** point 4, resolving **Q-055**; amended 2026-10-21). *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 5 and 6: the tenant `card_attempt.*` family is LIVE only; TEST mode writes zero tenant `audit_events`, denials included — a TEST denial RETURNED by a writing RPC writes only a private event retained 90 days; operator actions and security mismatches are private events of `app.card_provider_private_events`.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 8: the tenant audit carries tokens only — the `free_order` reason stays a sanitized ledger note; the Dashboard registry, safe-detail labels and ar / he / en titles of this family are the one Dashboard exception of [MVP_SCOPE](MVP_SCOPE.md) §5.)*
  8. **Receipt.** The POS prints its own localized receipt; no card line (brand, last four, approval number) is added in phase 1 (**Q-052**). *(Amended 2026-10-21: the interim receipt / legal policy and the accountant gate before the first LIVE charge are **D-053** point 8.)*
- **Alternatives considered:** mapping provider states onto `pending`/`tendered` payment rows (see **D-051**); a new tender value instead of `method='card'` with ledger provenance — rejected (the method CHECK, three label mappers and two report validators would change; the ledger join gives provenance); storing the provider token for future charges — rejected (widens PCI scope; tokenisation not purchased); printing the provider's receipt text — rejected for phase 1 (fixed language; carries merchant and terminal numbers).
- **Consequences:** (+) no float, no PAN, no hidden reversal, one identity end to end; (−) the `approved_unrecorded` case needs an operational procedure before any live charge; (−) the status-lookup semantics for an id that never reached the provider, a declined attempt and a still-pending pad are discovered in TEST mode, inside the owner-sealed non-settling TEST facility (**D-051** point 7; **Q-048**) — until then "not found" is UNKNOWN and an order is released for a new attempt only after the manager's confirmation-bound `free_order` (**D-053** point 1).
- **Related:** **D-051**; [MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §1 rule 6, §2, §8, §11, §12.3, §17; [STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §5; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §12, TH-8, T-023; [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-047..Q-057; **D-053**; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md). **D-056** (status and points 1, 2, 4, 5 and 7 amended 2026-10-22, round 4). **D-057** (points 3, 6 and 7 amended 2026-10-23, round 5).

## DECISION D-053 — BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001: operational, device, credential, recovery and retention policy for the card-present pilot (owner decisions of 2026-10-21)

- **Status:** Owner-decided on 2026-10-21 (the command-room approval of the 001A / 001B push and the command-room PR review of #363); recorded here as **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9) pending independent Codex review and human merge approval; takes the next free sequential ID **D-053**. Resolves **Q-051**, **Q-053**, **Q-054**, **Q-055**, **Q-056** and **Q-057**; **Q-052** stays Accepted Open with the interim engineering policy of point 8. Claims no production readiness; every hosted step keeps its own owner approval.
- **Context:** **D-051** / **D-052** left the recovery of an approved-but-unrecorded charge, the plan tier, the per-tenant credential model, TEST-mode traces, the pilot till platform and the hand-recorded card tender as open owner questions, and the first provider contract ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55, PR #363) granted `authenticated` callers the ledger mutations and relied on client-side refusal while a card claim was active. The command-room review of 2026-10-21 rejected both, and the owner decided the open questions.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Approved-but-unrecorded and orphan policy (Q-051; amended rounds 2 and 3, 2026-10-22).** A provider approval whose internal recording is pending is durable and recoverable; the only in-app recovery is completing the original `payment.create` → `record_payment` under the same immutable attempt / order identity on the original order — resent through the normal path when the first leg never reached the server, or re-dispatched through the **D-054** explicit manager Resume when the first leg was memoized with a recoverable refusal. The card is never charged again, the approval is never moved to another order, and the application performs no automatic provider void, refund or release. An approved attempt without its matching payment keeps its order claim — unless its order was already freed (an `order_freed` orphan, which blocks only its frozen shift's close, **D-056** point 3) — and is never freed by a manager. If the original payment can no longer be legally recorded — including when its frozen shift / drawer context cannot be restored (**D-055**) — the attempt is reported `orphan_approved`, every new tender, card charge, discount, edit, void and new provider attempt on that order stays refused while the standing charge is unresolved (except an `order_freed` orphan, whose freed order is never re-blocked: it blocks only its frozen shift's close while that shift is open — amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 3), and an owner / operator reconciliation is required; only documented provider confirmation that the charge was actually voided or refunded (`provider_voided` / `provider_refunded`) lets an operator resolve it and free the order, a same-day provider void being performed manually in the provider's back office only after explicit owner approval; a standing charge the owner wants to keep but RestoFlow cannot legally record stays an open incident until a future, separately designed, audited financial-adjustment workflow exists ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.7). No in-app reversal exists in phase 1 (**D-023** / **D-024** unchanged). An `unknown` or `released` attempt frees its order only through the manager's `free_order` after at least one provider lookup attempt (an engineering safeguard) and documented provider evidence that no approved charge exists — a `provider_report` or `provider_back_office` reference with a reason; poll-cap exhaustion, a provider outage or any self-attestation never frees an order (the **Q-048** interim, now bounded); a command-room override would be a future operator-only audited action and none ships in phase 1. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 2 and 3: a recording refused `permission_denied` or `revoked_employee` is resumed by an explicit `manager`+ Resume on the same device with no reinstatement — the manager becomes the payment actor and the ledger keeps the initiator; `revoked_device` is not recoverable; a dead or permanently revoked pilot device cannot record and its approvals are `orphan_approved`; a provider-reported charge anomaly is never manager-freed and ends only by a later exact result or the operator resolution.)*
  2. **Server-side exclusion while a claim is active.** While an order has an active claim ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3), no competing `payment.create` (any tender), discount, sent-order edit (`order.edit`, `order.items_add`) or void may commit; the only exception is the matching approved attempt's own `payment.create` identity. The proposed guard location (fixed by 001D) is `app.card_claim_guard`, called by `app.sync_push` in those five existing dispatch arms before the dispatched RPC and RETURNing the typed `payment_in_progress` (or `amount_mismatch` / `card_terminal_required`) *(amended 2026-10-22: the money arms also RETURN `card_context_lost`, and a sixth arm, `shift.close`, RETURNS `card_payment_in_progress` — **D-055**)*; the financial body of `app.record_payment` and the other four RPC bodies are unchanged; client snapshot gates are UX only ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3). *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 1 and 8: the claim first refuses `order_not_chargeable` / `order_already_settled` under the same `orders` lock; the guard defers a role the dispatched RPC never permits to that RPC's own `permission_denied`, returns attempt identifiers only to a session that may act on the attempt and keeps `detail` a scalar token; card-sensitive operations travel one per `sync_push` call and are never memoized as permanent on a transient database failure.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 1 and 8: the exception is the matching LIVE attempt's own admissible recording leg under the identity-first gate — a TEST identity never records; the guard first applies the dispatched RPC's own authorization and its refusals carry no attempt identifier, which supersedes "returns attempt identifiers only to a session that may act on the attempt".)*
  3. **Supabase plan (Q-053).** The live Supabase organization was verified as PRO by the owner; no plan or billing change is part of the pilot. Correctness still uses initiate-and-poll with a start deadline bounded by the 150 s request idle timeout and never depends on the maximum paid wall clock; a background continuation stays at most an optimization.
  4. **TEST-mode traces and retention (Q-055).** TEST attempts exist only in the private ledger with `mode='test'` and `order_id` NULL; they may be authorization-scoped to the pilot organization / restaurant / branch / device / employee but never write `payments`, receipts, shifts, order history, Dashboard revenue or the tenant Activity log (no tenant `audit_events` row at all, denials included — a TEST denial RETURNED by a writing RPC writes only a private event, **D-056** point 6). Only sanitized metadata is stored — attempt and correlation identifiers, scope ids, amount and currency, state transitions, the sanitized provider return class / code, a provider reference if safe, timestamps, build / version — never raw requests or responses, PAN, BIN, expiry, token, receipt text, test-card values or unrestricted payloads. Sanitized TEST rows carry `retention_until = created_at + 90 days` and an optional incident hold set by the operator-only audited RPC `app.card_attempt_set_incident_hold`; an operator-only, audited purge deletes only expired TEST rows not under hold and the expired `mode='test'` private events whose attempt, if any, is not under hold. Sanitized LIVE attempt evidence follows the normal financial / audit retention policy (**D-013**, **Q-005**): no phase-1 application role, RPC or the TEST purge deletes it. No raw provider or card data at any time. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 5 and 6: TEST mode writes zero tenant `audit_events`, authorization denials included — a TEST denial RETURNED by a writing RPC writes only a sanitized private event retained 90 days, and a read-only refusal or an invalid session writes no audit or private-event row; the hold and the purge are private operator events with a verified actor; TEST attempts are projected `test_<state>` and never as financial classifications; no phase-1 application role, RPC or the TEST purge deletes LIVE evidence — final legal retention per **D-013** / **Q-005**.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 4, 6 and 8: TEST rows store and project no card or provider-reference data — they keep the state, the return code and class, the approval type, the status code, the anomaly code and `charge_may_exist`, the amount and currency, the correlation identity and their lookup, release, hold, retention and time metadata; a TEST attempt that no lookup resolves is closed only by the operator resolution `test_closed`; a TEST revision requires a positive `max_test_amount_minor` (fail closed); a TEST private event can be held on its own; the hold and the purge name an attested owner approver.)*
  5. **Merchant of record and credentials (Q-054; amended round 2, 2026-10-22).** Each restaurant is the merchant of record and owns its acquirer / settlement agreement; BIZBOT never receives or settles restaurant funds. `terminal_ref` and `credential_ref` stay distinct opaque references. Every credential record in the configuration secret is mode- and scope-aware: a TEST record (`mode='test'`, `scope='development'`) belongs to the BIZBOT / Z-Credit development environment, not to any restaurant's merchant account, is usable only under the pinned Maps Burger TEST operator revision on the one designated device, and is never reused as LIVE; a LIVE record (`mode='live'`, `scope='merchant'`) carries the `restaurant_id` it belongs to, which must equal the proven restaurant — per restaurant, never shared across restaurants. The tenant / device mapping references records only by `credential_ref`, stores no credential value, and never claims that a development TEST credential is owned by the pilot restaurant. Secrets live only in Supabase server-side secret storage, in a configuration shape that holds several records without a schema replacement. Claim validation is mode-aware and fails closed: a cross-mode or cross-tenant reference refuses the charge ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.4 / §4.55.9, [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.5). *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 7: `status` and `release` use only the attempt's frozen references and re-validate the credential record's mode, scope and restaurant on every provider call; a credential record stays while any `sent` / `unknown` / `released` attempt uses it, and a rotation adds new references instead of re-pointing old ones; a mismatch with proven scope is a private security event.)*
  6. **One physical pilot till (Q-056).** Phase 1 is the Android POS APK on one explicitly designated Maps Burger POS device, enabled first and alone; web POS, browser tabs, kiosk and a second till are out of scope. The device UUID is selected and recorded in 001H, never hard-coded. The `payment_terminals` row is device-specific; no branch-level default may authorize a charge in phase 1 (that design is **DEFERRED**); the POS-local toggle is UX only — the server mapping row plus the operator revision are the authority. The pilot build is a dedicated signed build from the exact reviewed and merged source. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 4: the one-device rule is enforced server-side — at most one `payment_terminals` row of the pilot organization `enabled` or `draining` — and the binding is written only by the operator RPC `app.set_payment_terminal`.)*
  7. **Hand-recorded card on a terminal-enabled till (Q-057).** On a LIVE terminal-enabled pilot till the Card action always runs the integrated provider path; the legacy hand-recorded `card` tender is hidden on that till and refused server-side (`card_terminal_required`) unless the `payment.create` matches an approved provider attempt identity, whatever the state of the master flag or the operator revision. Tills without an enabled `live` mapping row keep the legacy behaviour during the rollout. A standalone terminal used during an outage is never silently recorded as an integrated card payment; its manager reconciliation is a later, separately audited workflow. There is no manual-card fallback on the phase-1 pilot till. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 1 and 4: the exemption covers only a LIVE approved attempt's own admissible recording leg under the identity-first gate; the refusal holds for a LIVE mapping in `configured`, `enabled` or `draining`, and only `retired` — after its gates — re-opens the hand-recorded tender.)*
  8. **Receipt and legal document gate (Q-052 interim).** The provider's terminal slip and the BIZBOT operational receipt are separate documents; BIZBOT prints and records "Card" only after the authoritative `record_payment` succeeds; no printing production change ships in phase 1; the BIZBOT receipt alone is never claimed to be a tax invoice or a legally sufficient payment voucher; a qualified accountant's confirmation of the Israeli tax / receipt document flow is mandatory before the first LIVE production charge and blocks nothing in 001D–001F.
  9. **Edge boundary posture (amended round 2, 2026-10-22).** The caller-JWT path of the Edge boundary is a read-only context proof plus the manager's audited `free_order` (the owner read `owner_card_attempts` is a separate `authenticated` PostgREST RPC, and the operator-only set has no app-role grant); every app-role-callable mutation that creates, claims or changes an attempt is a service-only RPC called by the adapter after that proof and re-proven server-side at write time (two implementer exceptions recorded for the owner's confirmation at review: the provider-fact writer `card_attempt_finish`, scope-bound to the row id and the organization, device, identity, mode, amount and currency of the same request, and the **D-054** `app.card_recording_resume_guard`, reached only through `app.sync_push` under the caller's own proven session and dispatching only for `manager`+, which writes only the resume counter, the refusal history and the Resume history); a direct PostgREST call by an `authenticated` client can never create a ledger row, claim an order, change provider state, mark a recording refused or request a release ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.2 / §4.55.10; the **D-045** service-only boundary precedent, narrowed to the adapter). *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 4, 5 and 10: the provider-fact writer is scope-bound to the row id, organization, device, identity, mode, amount and currency the adapter obtained in the same request; the Resume guard dispatches only for `manager`+ but appends a history entry for every request that reaches it for a live attempt; the manager's `free_order` is the third, caller-authority exception; operator actions require a verified operator actor and write private events.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 3 and 6: the Resume guard re-proves an active manager before any reopen, dispatch or retry and appends a history entry only on an authorized dispatch; operator actions name an attested owner approver and record the database executor.)*
  10. **Approved Work IDs.** `BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001G-DASHBOARD-VERIFICATION` (tests and verification only — prove `method='card'` reporting; a production Dashboard fix would be a separate Work ID), `BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001H-PILOT-OPS` (gates, operator allowlist, device binding, deployment and runbook, evidence, reconciliation, rollback and checklist; no live enablement or provider call without a later approval), `BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-HYGIENE-001` (a tracked `.gitignore` block for vendor-private paths and `tools/check_secrets.sh` provider / vendor deny patterns with tests, never adding, copying, inspecting or committing vendor evidence; independent of 001A / 001B) and `DASHBOARD-COLLECTED-LABEL-001` (the all-tender "Cash collected" label relabelled to a tender-neutral "Total collected" equivalent in the supported locales; l10n and focused tests only; not mixed into 001G). None of these implementations is folded into 001B.
- **Alternatives considered:** freeing an approved-unrecorded order for another tender — rejected (two charges for one order become possible; the approval must be re-recorded or reconciled); freeing on poll-cap exhaustion — rejected (an inconclusive lookup is not evidence that no charge exists); a branch-default terminal mapping in phase 1 — deferred (one till, one device, exact binding); one shared LIVE merchant credential — rejected (every tenant would charge one terminal, **D-001**); keeping the manual `card` tender on the enabled till — rejected (it weakens the "every card went through the terminal" guarantee and lets an outage charge be recorded as integrated); `authenticated` ledger mutations behind the SQL proof — rejected (a legitimate client could claim an order, set recording-refused metadata or mark a release without contacting the provider); a guard inside each RPC's preamble or a trigger — rejected (five bodies re-created, `record_payment`'s among them; a RAISE is flattened, [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.35).
- **Consequences:** (+) no double charge from a freed approval, no manual-card bypass on the pilot till, no cross-restaurant credential, no authenticated path to the ledger, no TEST row retained beyond 90 days without a hold; (−) `app.sync_push` is re-created once by 001D (six arms including the **D-055** `shift.close` exclusion, one guard call, plus the **D-054** reopen step), the service-role credential now authorizes the adapter's claims and marks (its compromise is already SEV-1, [OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY](OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY.md) §3.3), an orphaned approval blocks its order until an operator resolution (an `order_freed` orphan only its frozen shift's close, **D-056** point 3), and the accountant confirmation gates the first LIVE charge. **RISK R-002**, **R-003**, **R-007** and **R-008** are unchanged in direction and narrowed in scope. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 8: the single `app.sync_push` re-creation now carries four changes — the six-arm guard, the **D-054** reopen, the card-sensitive single-operation check and the card-sensitive transient classification.)*
- **Related:** **D-055** (points 1 and 2 of this decision amended 2026-10-22), **D-054** (the recording-recovery exception, 2026-10-22); **D-051** (points 2, 3 and 6 amended 2026-10-21), **D-052** (points 2, 3, 4, 5, 7 and 8 amended 2026-10-21 / 2026-10-22), **D-045**; [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.7, §4.14, §4.30b, §4.30c, §4.33, §4.35 item 4, §4.55; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.4 / §8.5; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §12 / TH-8 / T-023; [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18; [RUNBOOKS](RUNBOOKS.md) §8; [OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY](OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY.md) §3.2; [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-051..Q-057; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md). **D-056** (points 1, 2, 4, 5 and 9 and the consequences amended 2026-10-22, round 4). **D-057** (points 2, 4, 6, 7 and 9 amended 2026-10-23, round 5).

## DECISION D-054 — BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001: the narrow recording-recovery exception for a provider-approved card payment whose first recording leg was memoized as rejected

- **Status:** Owner-approved design at the command-room review round 2 of 2026-10-22 (PR #363); recorded here as **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9) pending independent Codex review and human merge approval; takes the next free sequential ID **D-054** (verified free on the branch and on `main` `ec134ded`). Shipped by 001D (server) and 001F (POS); no code exists yet.
- **Context:** The live `app.sync_push` terminal ledger replays an existing `sync_operations` row whose `status` is `applied`, `rejected`, `dead` or `conflict` and never dispatches it again; `app.record_payment`'s own success ledger (`order_operations`) is written only on success, but the outer `sync_operations` rejection is terminal and replayed. The first provider contract promised "clear the cause, then Resume the same `payment.create` identity", which that ledger makes impossible once the first recording leg has been memoized as rejected. A provider-approved charge must still be recorded exactly once under its immutable identity, without a second charge and without a new operation id.
- **Decision:**
  1. **One immutable business identity.** The provider charge and its payment share the `(device_id, local_operation_id)` of the durable POS PaymentAttempt. The provider charge is never repeated and the provider correlation keys never change.
  2. **The first recording leg.** The same immutable `payment.create` identity is pushed with `tender_type='card'`, `amount_tendered_minor` = the attempt amount and `expected_revision` omitted (NULL): kitchen and fulfilment status can legitimately move the order revision while the provider claim already blocks every money and order-content mutation, so the server's amount, currency and claim checks are the authority ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.7) *(amended 2026-10-22: the leg is admitted only inside the shift and drawer frozen at the claim, **D-055**)*.
  3. **Terminal replay unchanged.** `app.sync_push`'s terminal replay stays exactly as it is for every ordinary operation and for every non-provider payment (cash, hand-recorded card, `bit`, `external`).
  4. **The single carve-out.** A terminally `rejected` `payment.create` row may be reopened and redispatched under the SAME identity only when ALL of the following hold: a `live` provider attempt exists for the same organization, device, `local_operation_id` and order; its state is `approved` and no matching `payments` row exists; the attempt is not freed or resolved and its claim is still active; the order is still in an eligible, chargeable state; the attempt's frozen shift is still OPEN and its frozen drawer session still ACTIVE on the same device (**D-055**, amended 2026-10-22 — a Resume can never switch financial context); the current order amount and currency still equal the approved attempt's; the refusal was reported through `status` and recorded on the attempt with the same token the sync row stores; the current actor holds `manager`-or-higher authority on the same exact device; the resent operation's type and payload fingerprint are identical to the stored row's; the stored refusal is in the explicit recoverable allowlist `precondition_failed`, `revoked_employee`, `permission_denied` — read from the stored sync fields and result tokens, never from a client assertion (`revoked_device` is never recoverable, **D-056** point 2); and the request is an explicit `manager`+ Resume recording (the additive envelope key `recording_resume` carrying the resume count the manager acted on), never an automatic retry. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 2: the recoverable allowlist is `precondition_failed`, `revoked_employee` and `permission_denied`; `revoked_device` is removed — no implemented same-pairing reactivation path exists in phase 1 — the device record must be active, and the order must also be unsettled by any other payment.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 3: the actor passes the active-manager proof — a valid PIN session, an active membership covering the branch, an active employee profile, a `manager` / `restaurant_owner` / `org_owner` role, the same exact active POS device; the denials `actor_not_active`, `not_manager` and `device_not_active` never increment the count, and a reopened row back at `pending` is dispatched only under that proof.)*
  5. **Atomic reopen, one dispatch.** The exception atomically reopens the SAME `sync_operations` row to `in_flight`, increments a bounded recording-resume counter on the private provider ledger row, preserves the prior sanitized refusal in that ledger and in the audit event, and dispatches once under the row locks; a Resume whose `recording_resume` value differs from the stored count is refused `resume_stale`, so concurrent Resumes dispatch exactly once. It never calls Z-Credit and never changes provider state. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 10: `recording_resume_count` increments only on an authorized dispatch; `recording_resume_history` appends a sanitized entry for every Resume request that reaches the guard for a live attempt, refused ones included; a refused request never changes provider state or the claim; the reopen sits only in the valid-device ledger-claim step of `app.sync_push`, never in the revoked-device path.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 3: `recording_resume_history` appends an entry only on an authorized dispatch, at most three; a refused request never grows it — a refused request of an active manager is audited once per attempt, Resume count and denied reason — and supersedes the 2026-10-22 "refused ones included" rule.)*
  6. **Never reopened.** `conflict`, `order_not_chargeable`, `amount_mismatch`, `payment_in_progress`, `card_terminal_required`, `revoked_device` (**D-056** point 2), an illegal or terminal order state, `card_context_lost` (reason `context_lost` — a frozen shift / drawer context that cannot be restored, **D-055**; amended 2026-10-22), an unknown or generic refusal that is not proven recoverable, and any attempt that already has a `payments` row (replayed as recorded). These stay `approved_unrecorded` or become `orphan_approved` for reconciliation (**D-053** point 1). *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 2 and 10: `revoked_device` is never reopened; every non-recoverable case becomes `orphan_approved`, with the reason precedence `order_freed` > `context_lost` > `order_terminal` > `amount_mismatch` > `device_revoked` > `resume_limit` > `nonrecoverable_refusal` > `recording_refused` > `pending_recording`.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 1, 7 and 8: `provider_recording_refused` and `test_attempt_not_recordable` are never reopened; a stored token outside the closed list is `nonrecoverable_refusal`; the reason precedence gains `device_session_unavailable` between `recording_refused` and `pending_recording`.)*
  7. **POS journal.** The POS provider sibling journal keeps the base PaymentAttempt unresolved as `approved_unrecorded`; it never converts the business attempt into a terminal refusal. A manager-only explicit Resume recording resends the same immutable `local_operation_id`, target, client timestamp, operation type and payload / fingerprint, the only additive control difference being the fingerprint-excluded `recording_resume` envelope key (the whole serialized envelope is not required to be byte-identical; amended 2026-10-22) — no new charge, no new provider attempt, no new operation id. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 10: `recording_resume` is a sibling top-level key of the operation object beside `payload`, never inside it, and the Resume is pushed as the only operation of its call.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 2: the provider-leg sibling record holds the canonical recording operation, persisted before its first send and serialized without `expected_revision`; a never-delivered leg may be resent by any active cashier+ on the same device; once a `rejected` sync row exists, only the manager Resume applies.)*
  8. **Audit and cap.** The audit action `card_attempt.recording_resume_requested` (safe detail: attempt state, prior refusal token, resume count, outcome, denied reason) records every Resume request that reaches the guard for a live attempt, dispatched or refused ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.33). The phase-1 maximum is **3** explicit Resumes per attempt; on exhaustion the order stays blocked and operator reconciliation is required. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 10: every request that reaches the guard for a live attempt is also appended to the ledger's `recording_resume_history`.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 3: Resume evidence is bounded — `recording_resume_history` holds authorized dispatches only (at most three); a refused request of an active manager is audited once per attempt, Resume count and denied reason; a session failing the active-member proof writes nothing attempt-specific; this supersedes the "every Resume request that reaches the guard" rule above and its 2026-10-22 history note.)*
  9. **001D scope.** 001D may re-create `app.sync_push` once, for exactly the changes named here and in **D-056** point 8 (four in all): the claim guard already approved (**D-053** point 2; six arms — the five money arms plus the **D-055** `shift.close` arm) and this one provider-approved recording-reopen exception in the valid-device path's ledger-claim step for `payment.create` rows (the revoked-device path replays unchanged). It must prove that the rest of `app.sync_push` is byte / semantic identical and that the `app.record_payment` body hash is unchanged ([DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18.2) *(amended 2026-10-22, **D-055**: the sixth arm)*. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 8: the single re-creation now carries four changes — the guard, this reopen, the card-sensitive single-operation check and the card-sensitive transient classification — and the rest stays byte / semantic identical.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 3: the reopen change also carries the protected `recording_resume` parse — in the valid-device path's per-operation preamble, before the fingerprint step, refusing a non-empty `depends_on` beside the key — and the `resume_authority_required` check in the ledger-claim step's adopt branch for a `pending` row; the revoked-device path ignores the key and stays unchanged; still four changes.)*
  10. **Required tests** ([TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6, T-023): ordinary rejected sync operations still replay forever; ordinary, manual-card and cash payments never get the exception; a seeded memoized `precondition_failed` token of a provider-approved attempt, with its frozen shift still OPEN and its frozen drawer still ACTIVE, is resumed once by a manager and records exactly once (amended 2026-10-22, **D-056**: a repair by a new shift or drawer is `context_lost`, **D-055**); no automatic retry; a non-manager Resume is refused; a changed payload or fingerprint is refused; amount or currency drift is refused; an attempt whose payment already exists replays recorded and never redispatches; the resume-count cap; concurrent Resumes dispatch once; the provider API call count is unchanged during recording resumes; the recording leg and the Resume recording omit `expected_revision` and record after `orders.revision` moved, while a stale non-NULL `expected_revision` is memoized `conflict` and never reopened. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056**: a `permission_denied` recording resumed once by a manager; `revoked_device` never resumable; a forced deadlock on the recording leg answered `transient_failure` and recorded once on resend; a mixed batch refused `single_operation_required` with nothing memoized; a refused Resume appending history without changing the count.)* *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057**: plus the revoked-membership and inactive-employee (`actor_not_active`), expired-PIN (the unchanged preamble's whole-call 42501), revoked-device and transient-resend Resume cases, the bounded-evidence cases — a refused Resume no longer appends history; it changes neither the count nor the history and is audited once per attempt, count and denied reason — and the malformed `recording_resume` cases; the provider-call assertion belongs to the Edge lane — pgTAP proves the attempt's provider state unchanged.)*
- **Alternatives considered:** a new operation id for the retry — rejected (it breaks the one-identity rule and the duplicate-check derivation, **D-052** point 2); leaving the rejection terminal and reconciling every repaired precondition by hand — rejected (an approved charge whose frozen shift is still open but whose recording was refused for a lapsed membership or a non-cashier session would stay unrecorded for a procedural reason — amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 2: a revoked or suspended pairing is not recoverable); reopening any rejected row — rejected (only a proven-recoverable refusal on a provider-approved attempt may redispatch). *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 7: it is a recording memoized `revoked_device` that is not recoverable; a pairing loss with no memoized refusal is the non-orphan `device_session_unavailable`, and the leg records once the same device record is re-paired.)*
- **Consequences:** (+) an approved charge survives a lapsed membership (`revoked_employee`) or a non-cashier PIN session (`permission_denied`) through an explicit manager Resume, inside its still-open frozen shift, without a second charge or a hand-made operation (amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 2: a recording memoized `revoked_device` is `orphan_approved`) *(amended 2026-10-22, **D-055**: a closed frozen shift is `context_lost`, never resumable)*; (−) `app.sync_push` gains one more narrowly scoped change in 001D, proven byte / semantic identical elsewhere; (−) a non-recoverable memoized refusal leaves the attempt to the orphan procedure. **RISK R-002** narrowed; **R-003**, **R-007**, **R-008** unchanged.
- **Related:** **D-055** (the frozen shift / drawer context every Resume must keep), **D-053** (points 1 and 2; cross-referenced), **D-052** point 2, **D-022**, **D-036**; [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.7, §4.14, §4.33, §4.55.3, §4.55.7, §4.55.10; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.4; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) T-023; [RUNBOOKS](RUNBOOKS.md) §8; [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md). **D-056** (points 4–10, the alternatives and the consequences amended 2026-10-22, round 4). **D-057** (points 4–10 and the alternatives amended 2026-10-23, round 5).

## DECISION D-055 — BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001: a LIVE card attempt freezes its shift, drawer and initiating actor; same-context recording; shift-close exclusion; one canonical lock order

- **Status:** Required by the final independent review of 2026-10-22 (PR #363, REQUEST_CHANGES); recorded here as **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9) pending the final Codex approval pass and human merge approval; takes the next free sequential ID **D-055** (verified free on the branch and on `main` `ec134ded`). Shipped by 001D (server) and 001F (POS); no code exists yet.
- **Context:** `app.record_payment` stamps whichever OPEN shift and ACTIVE drawer session exist on the paying device at recording time (`shift_id`, `cash_drawer_session_id`, selected FOR UPDATE after the order lock). The provider contract started a LIVE attempt only when such a shift and drawer existed but froze neither of them, did not stop `shift.close` while the terminal interaction or the internal recording was unresolved, and let the **D-054** recovery follow a "reopened" shift — so a charge initiated in shift A could be recorded in shift B or on a later business day. That is not acceptable: the shift and drawer are the rollup boundary of sales and cash reporting (**D-028**, [MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §14).
- **Decision:**
  1. **Frozen context at the LIVE claim.** `app.card_attempt_claim` derives from the SQL proof and persists on the attempt: `shift_id` (the device's OPEN shift), `cash_drawer_session_id` (that shift's ACTIVE drawer session), `initiated_by_employee_profile_id` and `initiated_by_membership_id`; `card_attempt_finish` stamps `provider_approved_at` when the approval is learned (commit reply or lookup). TEST attempts keep `order_id`, `shift_id` and `cash_drawer_session_id` NULL; the initiating employee / membership is frozen in both modes (TEST attempts are employee-scoped, **D-053** point 4). None of these identifiers is ever taken from the client or from the Edge payload. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 1: before freezing anything the claim refuses, under the same `orders` lock, an order that is not chargeable or already settled by any tender.)*
  2. **Same-context recording.** The first recording leg and every **D-054** Resume recording pass to `record_payment` only while the exact frozen `shift_id` is still OPEN and the exact frozen `cash_drawer_session_id` is still ACTIVE on the same device and branch — exactly the rows `record_payment` would stamp. A newly opened shift or a newly created drawer session never absorbs an earlier provider approval. `app.record_payment`'s financial body stays unchanged. When the frozen context cannot be validly restored, the attempt is `orphan_approved` (reason `context_lost`), the order stays blocked, and only the existing provider void / refund reconciliation resolves it ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.7).
  3. **Shift-close exclusion.** `shift.close` is dispatched only through `app.sync_push` → `app.close_shift` (no public wrapper, no other caller — verified in the migrations and asserted by T-023 for `app.close_shift` and `app.reconcile_shift`), so the guard lives in that arm: `app.card_claim_guard` receives the payload `shift_id` (as `p_shift_id`, resolved under the proof's organization and branch exactly as `close_shift` does) and RETURNS the typed `card_payment_in_progress` while any LIVE attempt whose frozen `shift_id` is that shift — whatever device pushes the close — is `sent`, `unknown` or `released` with `order_freed_at` NULL, `approved` without its payment (recoverable or not) or `orphan_approved` without a `provider_voided` / `provider_refunded` resolution. An `unknown` / `released` attempt already freed by the manager's provider-evidence `free_order` does not block (an approval found later makes it an `order_freed` orphan, which blocks the close of its frozen shift while that shift is open and is a reconciliation incident after it closed); declined attempts, recorded attempts and attempts resolved with documented `provider_voided` / `provider_refunded` do not block. `reconcile_shift` requires a `closed` shift, so reconciliation is unreachable while the close is refused. TEST attempts carry no shift and never block a production shift. No UI rule stands in for this guard. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** points 3 and 8: one canonical predicate owned by [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3 — an unresolved provider anomaly also blocks; an approval or anomaly found after an evidence-based free blocks only while the frozen shift is open and is a reconciliation incident after it closed (nothing re-blocks a closed shift); the pilot POS closes online only, so no provisional local close exists; T-023 also proves that nothing but `app.sync_push` calls `app.close_shift` and nothing else writes `shifts.status='closed'`.)*
  4. **Canonical lock order.** Every path that combines these rows takes them in the order `sync_operations` (when present) → `orders` → `shifts` → `cash_drawer_sessions` → `card_provider_attempts`, or safely skips forward through it, never backward: the first claim (`orders` FOR UPDATE → `shifts` FOR UPDATE → `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE → ledger insert); the first recording leg (the sync row → `orders` FOR UPDATE in the guard → `shifts` and `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE in the guard's frozen-context check, re-locked by the unchanged `record_payment`); the **D-054** Resume (the sync row FOR UPDATE → `orders` → `shifts` FOR UPDATE → `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE → the ledger row); `card_attempt_mark_recording_result` (the sync row FOR UPDATE → the attempt row FOR UPDATE), `card_attempt_finish` and the status writes (the attempt row only); `free_order` and `card_attempt_resolve_orphan` (`orders` → the ledger row); `shift.close` (its own sync row → `shifts` FOR UPDATE in the guard → a non-locking read of the ledger rows frozen to that shift → `shifts` re-locked and `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE inside the unchanged `close_shift`). No path upgrades a lock it already holds: every path that may later call `record_payment` in the same transaction takes `shifts` and `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE from the start (`record_payment` re-locks them FOR UPDATE), so two concurrent Resumes for different attempts frozen to the same shift serialize instead of deadlocking. A concurrent claim and shift close, or a concurrent Resume and shift close, serialize on the `shifts` row and the later one sees the committed outcome (a `sent` row refuses the close; a closed shift refuses the claim or the Resume), so each pair is serial and deterministic; no path takes an earlier row after a later one, so no cycle exists between the Resume, the recording-result mark, the provider finish / status writes, `shift.close` and the first claim. 001D proves it with a lock-order stress test that includes two concurrent Resumes for different attempts frozen to the same shift. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056** point 8: exact modes — `sync_operations`, `orders`, `shifts`, `cash_drawer_sessions` and `card_provider_attempts` all FOR UPDATE, then `branch_receipt_counters` inside `record_payment` only; the guarantee is per transaction, so card-sensitive operations travel one per `sync_push` call; the full per-path list is owned by [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3.)*
  5. **Reporting and audit.** `owner_card_attempts` projects the frozen `shift_id`, `cash_drawer_session_id`, `initiated_by_employee_profile_id`, `initiated_by_membership_id`, `provider_approved_at` and the Resume history (who requested each Resume) to authorized owner / manager / `accountant` readers. The `payments` row records the actor who completes a Resume recording (the manager's PIN session, as `record_payment` already does), while the private ledger preserves who initiated the provider charge and who requested each Resume. No client-visible response exposes a credential or terminal reference. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 3: the payment actor is the active manager who completes `record_payment`; the ledger keeps the original initiator and each authorized Resume requester.)*
  6. **Required tests** ([TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6, T-023): `shift.close` refused during `sent` / `unknown` / `released` (with `order_freed_at` NULL) / approved-unrecorded / orphan-approved; shift close allowed after declined, recorded, `provider_voided`, `provider_refunded` and a provider-evidence `free_order` of an `unknown` / `released` attempt; another or a new shift or drawer cannot record the old approval; the exact frozen shift and drawer record once when valid; a **D-054** Resume cannot switch financial context; a concurrent claim versus shift close is serial and deterministic; a concurrent Resume versus shift close is serial and deterministic; a lock-order stress test shows no deadlock; TEST attempts never block a production shift. *(Amended 2026-10-22, **D-056**: also two concurrent Resumes for different attempts frozen to one shift, a recording-result mark versus a Resume of the same identity, both interleavings of a provider finish / status write and a close (not lock-serialized: the close decides on the committed state it reads), an `order_freed` orphan and an unresolved anomaly blocking the close while the shift is open, an after-close approval leaving the closed shift untouched, and the mixed-batch refusal.)*
- **Alternatives considered:** letting `record_payment` stamp the current shift (today's behaviour) — rejected (a charge would migrate across shifts or business days); a UI-only shift-close warning — rejected (a stale till or a queued offline close would bypass it); freezing the shift but allowing a manager to re-bind an approval to a new shift — rejected (that is exactly the migration this decision forbids; a lost context goes to the provider void / refund path).
- **Consequences:** (+) a shift's card totals and expected cash are complete at close, and an approval can never land in a later shift; (−) an unresolved LIVE attempt holds the shift open until it is resolved, so the runbook gains the "shift close refused" step, and a kept standing charge (an orphan the owner will not void or refund) holds that shift and station open until the future audited financial-adjustment workflow exists; (−) `app.card_claim_guard` gains a sixth arm and the ledger four frozen identifiers. **RISK R-002** and **R-008** narrowed; **R-003** unchanged.
- **Related:** **D-053** (points 1 and 2 amended 2026-10-22), **D-054** (points 2, 4, 6, 7 and 9 amended 2026-10-22), **D-028**, **D-022**; [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.7, §4.9, §4.14, §4.35 item 4, §4.55.2, §4.55.3, §4.55.4, §4.55.7, §4.55.10; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.2, §8.4; [STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §6.2; [MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §14; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) TH-8, T-023; [RUNBOOKS](RUNBOOKS.md) §8; [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18.2; [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md). **D-056** (points 1, 3, 4 and 6 amended 2026-10-22, round 4). **D-057** (point 5 amended 2026-10-23, round 5).

## DECISION D-056 — BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001: final-review round-4 owner decisions — paid-order pre-call guard, recoverable-recording and anomaly policies, scope-bound finish, private operator / security events, one TEST audit rule, credential revalidation and card-sensitive sync safety

- **Status:** Owner-decided at the command-room correction round 4 of 2026-10-22 on the final review of PR #363 (REQUEST_CHANGES; that review ran in Claude Code, so the independent Codex gate remains open); recorded here as **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9) pending the independent Codex review and human merge approval; takes the next free sequential ID **D-056** (verified free on the branch and on `main` `fe78bbcd`). Shipped by 001D (server), 001E (Edge) and 001F (POS); no code exists yet.
- **Context:** The final review found that the LIVE claim could start a provider charge on an order already settled by another tender; that a recording leg pushed under a non-cashier PIN session would strand a valid approval; that a provider-reported charge anomaly had no exit and could be freed by a manager; that the provider-fact writer's "scope" recheck had nothing to compare; that operator-only actions were specified as tenant audit rows with no verified actor; that TEST denials and TEST approvals leaked into tenant audit and financial classifications; that later provider calls skipped the credential mode / scope / restaurant checks; and a set of batching, transient-failure, locking, refusal-shape and wording gaps.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Paid-order pre-call guard (P0).** Before a LIVE claim row or any provider request, `app.card_attempt_claim` — under the `orders` row it locks FOR UPDATE, the same lock `app.record_payment` takes first, so the two serialize — refuses `order_not_chargeable` when `orders.grand_total_minor <= 0` and `order_already_settled` when a live `completed` `payments` row of any method exists for the order or `app.order_is_fully_settled` is true — after the idempotent replay of an existing identity (answered from the ledger) and before every other live precondition, whatever the order's status or revision: no ledger row, no provider request. The `expected_revision` check and the POS snapshot are never substitutes ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.2). *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 1 and 8: both checks run as separate statements after the `orders` FOR UPDATE has returned, under the held lock; an identity already used by another operation is refused `conflict` / `identity_in_use` before them.)*
  2. **Recoverable recording policy.** The **D-054** recoverable allowlist is `precondition_failed`, `revoked_employee` and `permission_denied`; `revoked_device` is removed — no implemented same-pairing reactivation path exists in phase 1 — and a recording memoized `revoked_device` becomes `orphan_approved`. A recording refused `permission_denied` (pushed under a non-cashier, kitchen or accountant PIN session) or `revoked_employee` is resumed only by an explicit `manager`+ Resume on the same exact device, never automatically; the original employee is never reinstated for it: the manager completing the same immutable payment identity becomes the `payments` actor while the ledger keeps the initiator. A dead or permanently revoked pilot device cannot record: a dead device is first revoked through the Dashboard's `revoke_device_management`, which deactivates the device record permanently, so its approvals classify `device_revoked`, become `orphan_approved` and need a provider void / refund — no cross-device recording in phase 1. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 3 and 7: the manager Resume requires the active-manager proof; `device_revoked` means a management-inactive device record, which is sticky, and a pairing loss alone is the non-orphan reason `device_session_unavailable`.)*
  3. **Provider anomaly.** A status lookup that indicates charge risk but cannot map exactly to approved or declined — an amount or currency mismatch, a pre-authorization, a partial refund, a refund or void status inconsistent with the ledger, any other out-of-contract charge-bearing status or an amount lexeme outside the grammar — keeps the state (a `sent` row moves to `unknown`; `released` stays `released`), sets the allowlisted `provider_anomaly_code` and `charge_may_exist = true` and — for a LIVE attempt — is classified `provider_anomaly` (a TEST attempt keeps the code as evidence only, point 6): the manager's `free_order` is forbidden, the order stays claimed and the frozen shift cannot close while open; only a later exact valid approved result, a later exact declined result, or the operator resolution backed by documented `provider_voided` / `provider_refunded` resolves it. An anomaly or approval found after an evidence-based free never re-blocks the freed order; it blocks the close of its frozen shift while that shift is open, and after the shift closed it is a reconciliation incident only; such an anomaly still ends by a later exact result (an exact approval making it an `order_freed` orphan) or the operator resolution.
  4. **Scope-bound finish.** The service-only provider-fact writer `card_attempt_finish` receives, besides the ledger row's server id, the `organization_id`, `device_id`, immutable `local_operation_id`, `mode`, `amount_minor` and `currency_code` the adapter obtained from the claim or the adapter read in the same trusted request (never from the POS) and writes only when the row matches all of them (`conflict` / `attempt_scope_mismatch` and a private security event otherwise); closed transitions, the allowlisted provider fields, the idempotent no-op on a lost race and the no-raw-payload rule are unchanged.
  5. **Private operator / security events.** A private immutable `app.card_provider_private_events` store (forced RLS, no client or table grants, never projected into the tenant Activity log, never a sync entity; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.6) records every operator action — the pilot revision, the orphan resolution, the TEST incident hold, the TEST purge — every TEST denial RETURNED by a writing RPC and every security mismatch (a credential mismatch found by the claim or the adapter read, a finish scope mismatch); the read-only context proof, adapter read and owner read write no denial evidence. Operator actions require a non-empty `operator_reference` and an `actor_app_user_id` verified as an active `org_owner` / `restaurant_owner` membership in the pilot scope. An unauthenticated or invalid-session request writes no ledger, audit or private-event row (no safe scope). LIVE cashier / manager actions with a proven employee keep the tenant `card_attempt.*` audit family. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 6: the operator actor is an attested owner approver — an existence-checked active owner membership, not an authenticated identity — and the database executor is recorded as `executor_principal`; the terminal binding (`operator.payment_terminal_updated`) and the TEST closure (`operator.card_attempt_test_closed`, **D-057** point 4) are operator actions.)*
  6. **One TEST audit rule and the TEST classification.** TEST mode writes zero tenant `audit_events` / Activity-log entries, denials included; a TEST denial RETURNED by a writing RPC may write only a sanitized private event retained 90 days like the TEST evidence. `approved_unrecorded`, `orphan_approved`, `recoverable`, `provider_anomaly`, recording warnings and the financial runbook queues apply to `mode='live'` only; a TEST attempt is projected `test_<state>` (an approved one `test_approved`) with `recorded=false`, never blocks a shift, never enters an owner financial report and is read only through the explicit TEST filter of the owner read. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 1: a defective `payment.create` of a TEST identity can leave only the rejected `sync_operations` row and its generic `sync.operation_rejected` audit, plus — only when the dispatched RPC denies the session's role — that RPC's own denial audit (`payment.denied`), and never a card-attempt audit.)*
  7. **Credential revalidation.** `status` and `release` use only the attempt's frozen `terminal_ref`, `credential_ref`, mode and restaurant, re-validate the current configuration records on every provider call (TEST: `mode='test'`, `scope='development'`; LIVE: `mode='live'`, `scope='merchant'` and the attempt's restaurant; the environment matching the mode) and never select a new mapping after rotation; a missing or mismatched record answers `provider_unavailable` with no provider call and leaves the attempt unresolved; a credential record stays in the configuration while any `sent` / `unknown` / `released` attempt uses it, one freed by `free_order` included (an `approved` or `declined` attempt is read without the re-validation), and a rotation adds new references instead of re-pointing old ones; the re-validation runs inside `card_attempt_adapter_read` from the adapter-supplied maps, which writes the private security event on a mismatch; a credential revoked at the provider keeps its record, so its lookups fail as ordinary lookup failures and the evidence-based `free_order` or the operator resolution stays reachable. The kill switch, the revision and the mapping may stop new starts but never switch credentials under in-flight recovery. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 8: the configuration environment never switches while an attempt of either mode still needs it for a status lookup — a `sent`, `unknown` or `released` attempt without a `resolution`, one freed by `free_order` included; an attempt with a `resolution` is final and makes no provider call.)*
  8. **Card-sensitive sync safety.** In phase 1 `shift.close`, a `payment.create` whose identity is a provider attempt and any operation carrying `recording_resume` must be the only operation of its `sync_push` call; a mixed call is refused `single_operation_required` for every operation before any business mutation and without a ledger row — a batching error the POS answers by resending each operation alone. A transient database failure of such an operation — `40P01`, `55P03` or a database-raised `40001` — is never memoized as a permanent rejection: its business subtransaction rolls back and the same row returns to `pending` with the retryable `transient_failure` (the `dependency_not_ready` precedent), so the same identity is resent and dispatched once; the deterministic `40001` raises of the dispatched RPC itself (`record_payment`, `close_shift`), recognised by its message prefix, stay terminal `conflict`. Exact lock modes: `sync_operations` FOR UPDATE (when present), `orders` FOR UPDATE, `shifts` FOR UPDATE, `cash_drawer_sessions` FOR UPDATE, `card_provider_attempts` FOR UPDATE, then `branch_receipt_counters` inside the unchanged `record_payment` only. The claim guard defers a role the dispatched RPC never permits to that RPC's own `permission_denied` and denial audit, returns attempt identifiers only to a session that may act on the attempt, and every refusal `detail` is one scalar token with structured facts as sibling keys. `app.sync_push` is therefore re-created once with four changes and an otherwise byte / semantic identical body. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 3 and 8: `recording_resume` is honoured on `payment.create` only — the single-operation rule covers only a `payment.create` carrying it, and on any other operation type the key is refused `invalid_payload` for that operation alone, with no batch abort of its own (a call that also holds a card-sensitive operation — a `shift.close` or a provider-identity `payment.create` — is still refused `single_operation_required` as a whole); the transient classes are `08`, `40`, `53`, `55`, `57`, `58` and `XX`, except the dispatched RPC's own raises under the prefix allowlist `record_payment:` / `close_shift:`; the guard defers only where the dispatched RPC is certain to deny on its payload-independent gates and never on a check of its own, and its refusals carry no attempt identifier.)*
  9. **Money and identity details.** A required `max_live_amount_minor` on the operator revision gates LIVE starts (absent ⇒ `card_payments_disabled`; exceeded ⇒ `precondition_failed` / `max_live_amount_exceeded`); the inbound provider amount is accepted only as raw token text matching `^(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,8})\.[0-9]{2}$` and converted by integer arithmetic; "no floating-point arithmetic" (integer division and modulo are the prescribed derivation); the automatic poll cap is per attempt; `local_operation_id` is `text`, a canonical lowercase UUID v4 validated at the Edge boundary and by a ledger CHECK, never cast inside `app.sync_push`; `recorded` means strictly that the matching internal `payments` row exists, never a provider settlement or deposit. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** point 1: `recorded` is the strict join of identity, order, method, amount, currency and the frozen shift and drawer.)*
  10. **D-054 precision.** The reopen sits only in the valid-device ledger-claim step of `app.sync_push`; the revoked-device path never reopens. `recording_resume_count` increments only on an authorized dispatch; `recording_resume_history` appends a sanitized entry for every Resume request that reaches the guard for a live attempt, refused ones included (a mixed call, a revoked-device push, a preamble conflict and a pending-row resend never reach it, and a row with no live attempt is replayed with no `card_attempt.*` row; the existing `sync.operation_*` audit is unchanged); a refused Resume never changes provider state or the claim. Orphan reasons and precedence: `order_freed` > `context_lost` > `order_terminal` > `amount_mismatch` > `device_revoked` > `resume_limit` > `nonrecoverable_refusal` > `recording_refused` > `pending_recording`. The Resume identity is the same `local_operation_id`, target, client timestamp, operation type and payload / fingerprint; only the sibling `recording_resume` control key differs. *(Amended 2026-10-23, **D-057** points 3 and 7: Resume history holds authorized dispatches only; the reason precedence gains `device_session_unavailable` between `recording_refused` and `pending_recording`.)*
  11. **PR #362 wording.** `method='card'` rows are classified correctly on the intended money surfaces; the separate all-tender "Cash collected" label defect is not evidence that card is misclassified and stays with `DASHBOARD-COLLECTED-LABEL-001`. Merging #362 alone temporarily exposes the earlier design wording; #363 merges right after its own final approval, and the stacked review order is never skipped.
- **Alternatives considered:** relying on the `expected_revision` check at the claim — rejected (a caller holding the current revision passes); keeping `revoked_device` recoverable on the documented `suspended → active` path — rejected (no migration implements it); letting `free_order` release an anomaly — rejected (a charge may exist); writing operator events into tenant `audit_events` — rejected by owner choice (operator and TEST activity stays private evidence outside the tenant Activity log, as the `app.set_mapsburger_web_orders` operator precedent keeps it); treating every `40001` as transient — rejected for the deterministic idempotency-conflict and revision-conflict raises of `record_payment` and `close_shift`; a global single-operation rule — rejected (only card-sensitive operations need it; today's POS and KDS already push one operation per call).
- **Consequences:** (+) no provider charge on a settled order; no approval stranded by a session role; anomalies cannot be freed; finish writes are self-checking; operator actions are attributable; TEST activity never reaches tenant audit or financial classifications; credentials cannot drift under in-flight recovery; a transient failure never orphans an approval. (−) `app.sync_push`'s single 001D re-creation carries four changes and must prove the rest identical; a new private table; operator RPCs take an actor; a recording memoized `revoked_device` — including the rare stale device session on a still-active pairing — is an orphan that needs a provider void / refund. **RISK R-002**, **R-003**, **R-007** and **R-008** narrowed.
- **Related:** **D-052** (status, points 2, 4, 5 and 7 amended), **D-053** (points 1, 2, 4, 5 and 9 and the consequences amended), **D-054** (points 4–10 amended), **D-055** (points 1, 3, 4 and 6 amended), **D-051** (status), **D-013**, **D-026**, **D-028**; [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.7, §4.9.1, §4.14, §4.33, §4.35 item 4, §4.55; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.2, §8.4–§8.6; [STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §6.2, §10; [OFFLINE_SYNC_SPEC](OFFLINE_SYNC_SPEC.md) §8, §10.1; [MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §1 rule 6, §11, §12.3, §14, §17; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §12, TH-8, T-023; [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6; [RUNBOOKS](RUNBOOKS.md) §8; [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18; [OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY](OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY.md) §3.3, §6, §7.1; [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-048, Q-055; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md). **D-057** (points 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10 amended 2026-10-23, round 5).

## DECISION D-057 — BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001: correction round 5 owner decisions — identity-first provider recording gate, provider recording leg without `expected_revision`, active-manager Resume authority, terminal-binding lifecycle, claim freshness, attested operator approver and sticky management revocation

- **Status:** Owner-decided at the command-room correction round 5 of 2026-10-23 on the Claude Code read-only review of PR #363 (REQUEST_CHANGES: five majors, 25 minors, six notes); recorded here as **PROPOSED** under the architecture-change procedure ([AGENT_WORKFLOW.md](AGENT_WORKFLOW.md) §9) pending the independent Codex review (§9 step 4; this round's Claude Code reviews, run on the command room's instruction, do not replace it) and human merge approval; takes the next free sequential ID **D-057** (verified free on `main` `fe78bbcd` and on both branches). Shipped by 001D (server), 001E (Edge), 001F (POS) and 001H (operations); no code exists yet.
- **Context:** The review found that a provider identity could still be recorded once its claim had ended — freed, resolved, TEST, or with the live mapping disabled — in another shift and at the order's current total; that the specified POS recording path would freeze `expected_revision` into the recording leg and strand an approval after any kitchen status change; that the **D-054** Resume guard authorized on role alone, so a revoked manager with a still-valid PIN session could consume the Resume budget; that binding a till to a merchant credential and switching it to LIVE had no operator RPC, actor, event or one-device invariant; and that the claim RPC could not tell the adapter whether a `start` was a replay. It also found 25 minor gaps and six notes (point 8).
- **Decision:**
  1. **Identity-first provider recording gate.** For every `payment.create`, before any claim-state logic, `app.card_claim_guard` resolves whether `(organization_id, device_id, local_operation_id)` belongs to a provider attempt of either mode. No match: the ordinary tender rules apply — the active-claim exclusion and, for a card tender, the manual-card rule of point 4. A TEST attempt matches: always refused `test_attempt_not_recordable`. A LIVE attempt matches: admitted to `record_payment` only as its own authoritative recording leg, when all hold — `approved`; not freed; no `resolution`; `charge_may_exist = false`; its claim active; the payload `order_id` equal to the attempt's; `tender_type='card'`; the order's total and currency equal to the frozen `amount_minor` / `currency_code`; the frozen shift still OPEN and the frozen drawer still ACTIVE on the device; no `payments` row carrying the identity (only a strict-join row makes the attempt `recorded`). A total or currency drift answers `amount_mismatch`, a lost frozen context `card_context_lost`, anything else the typed, non-disclosing `provider_recording_refused` with one scalar `detail`; a provider identity never falls through to an ordinary `record_payment` dispatch, whatever the terminal mapping's lifecycle; the checks run in a fixed order (resolved, freed, anomaly, not approved, order, tender, existing payment, amount, context — [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3). The claim refuses an identity already used by another operation (`identity_in_use`), and a row of the identity that is not its own canonical leg makes the attempt `orphan_approved` (`nonrecoverable_refusal`), so no approval is ever stranded without the operator exit. `recorded` is the strict join — organization, device, `local_operation_id`, `order_id`, `method='card'`, amount, currency, the frozen `shift_id` and the frozen `cash_drawer_session_id` — so an identity alone never hides a standing charge ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.3).
  2. **Provider recording leg without `expected_revision`.** The claim's `expected_revision` serves only the pre-charge stale-till check; after approval the recording `payment.create` omits it — a narrow, provider-leg-only exception to the POS's PDR-003 revision gate (the POS payment-attempt rule that mints and sends a payment attempt only with an authoritative order revision as `expected_revision`). Amount, currency, claim, order, device and the frozen shift and drawer are the recording authority, so kitchen or fulfilment revision movement never orphans a valid approval. 001F defines a provider-approved recording path instead of reusing the generic `PaymentAttemptSender` path unchanged: the existing 26-key base `PaymentAttempt` schema stays as it is; a separately versioned provider-leg sibling record holds the canonical recording operation, persisted before its first send and serialized without `expected_revision`; the first send, every transport resend, restart recovery and the **D-054** Resume reuse the same immutable identity and canonical payload; every non-provider tender keeps PDR-003. A canonical recording operation that never reached the server may be resent by any active cashier+ on the same exact device (no provider call; the ledger keeps the initiator; the payment actor is whoever completes `record_payment`); once a `rejected` sync row exists, the **D-054** manager-only rules apply.
  3. **Active-manager Resume authority.** Every **D-054** reopen, every dispatch of a reopened row and every transport retry of it re-proves a valid PIN session (first enforced by the unchanged `app.sync_push` preamble, which refuses an expired or ended session for the whole call), an active membership covering the branch, an active employee profile, a `manager` / `restaurant_owner` / `org_owner` role and the same exact POS device with an active device record. A failing proof is a typed denial — `actor_not_active`, `not_manager` or `device_not_active` — that never increments the Resume count and never dispatches; a reopened row back at `pending` after a transient failure answers any other session `resume_authority_required` (row kept `pending`, retryable, nothing dispatched), so a cashier never adopts or completes it. `permission_denied`, `revoked_employee` and `precondition_failed` stay recoverable only this way; `revoked_device` stays non-recoverable. The payment actor is the active manager who completes `record_payment`; the ledger keeps the original initiator and each authorized Resume requester. Resume evidence is bounded: `recording_resume_history` holds authorized dispatches only (at most three); a refused request never grows it; a session failing the active-member proof, or without a `manager`+ role, writes nothing attempt-specific; a refused manager request is audited once per attempt, Resume count and denied reason. The sibling `recording_resume` key is honoured only on `payment.create`, read by a protected integer parse (`0..3`; at 3 the guard answers `resume_limit` before any `resume_stale` check) before the ledger-claim and fingerprint steps; on another operation type, malformed, or beside a non-empty `depends_on`, it is a typed per-operation `invalid_payload` with no ledger mutation and no batch abort, and it never changes another RPC's transient classification.
  4. **Terminal binding lifecycle.** The operator-only `app.set_payment_terminal(...)` (final signature fixed by 001D; no wrapper, no app-role grant) is the only writer of `app.payment_terminals`.
