It requires an attested owner approver (point 6), a non-empty `operator_reference` pointing to the sealed owner approval and a reason, records the database executor, validates the exact active POS device and the pilot scope, and sets only opaque `terminal_ref` / `credential_ref` and the mode — never a raw credential or terminal identifier. Each row moves `configured → enabled | retired`, `enabled → draining` and `draining → enabled | retired`: `enabled` admits new integrated starts, `draining` stops them while every unresolved attempt finishes, `retired` is final; a device has at most one row that is not `retired`, so the LIVE row is created only after the TEST row is `retired`. At most one row of the pilot organization may be `enabled` or `draining` (a partial unique constraint). For a LIVE row in `configured`, `enabled` or `draining`, `card_terminal_mode='live'` is reported and the server `card_terminal_required` rule stays on — stopping new starts never restores the hand-recorded Card tender; only `retired` does, and retirement requires no unresolved provider attempt, no pending legacy card attempt, an empty relevant sync backlog and sealed owner approval — except inside the single atomic same-mode replacement call of a credential rotation ([API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.55.9: the `draining` row retired and the new `configured` row created in one transaction, the till never re-opening the hand-recorded tender), which needs neither these gates nor the creation gate; the new row still passes the activation gate before `enabled`. Because the till refuses the hand-recorded Card tender from `configured` on, creating a LIVE row requires no pending or in-flight `payment.create` of the device and sealed evidence that no legacy card attempt is pending on the till. "Unresolved" means a LIVE attempt classified `in_progress`, `provider_anomaly`, `approved_unrecorded` or `orphan_approved`, or a TEST attempt still `sent`, `unknown` or `released` with no `resolution`; a TEST attempt that no status lookup resolves is closed only by the operator resolution `test_closed` (attested owner approver, sealed reference to the provider's test-environment evidence, one private event, no order or money effect), so a failed TEST run never blocks the gates. Entering LIVE `enabled` requires the activation gates of [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18.1 step 9 — no pending or unconfirmed legacy card attempt on the till, no relevant `payment.create` backlog or ambiguity, no unresolved TEST or LIVE attempt, the exact reviewed pilot build installed, a known shift state, sealed evidence; a failed gate means no LIVE transition. Every change writes the private event `operator.payment_terminal_updated` (mode, prior and new lifecycle, start posture, attested approver, executor principal, reason code, operator reference — never the references); 001H performs no direct table edit.
  5. **Claim freshness.** `card_attempt_claim` returns, atomically with its decision, `claim_created`, `replayed`, the current state and the server row id with the safe adapter projection. Only the request whose transaction inserted the fresh `sent` row makes the provider commit call; an idempotent replay or a concurrent loser (its insert found the identity present) makes zero commit calls and answers or polls the existing attempt.
  6. **Attested operator approver.** Operator-only SQL actions are not JWT-authenticated and are never described as such: the operator RPCs take `p_actor_app_user_id`, checked to be an active `org_owner` / `restaurant_owner` membership in the pilot scope (an existence check, not authentication), and a sealed `operator_reference`, and record the database executor separately as `session_user`, captured inside the definer body (where `current_user` is the function owner). Private events carry `actor_kind='attested_owner_approver'` and an `executor_principal`. A JWT-backed operator interface would be a separate Work ID.
  7. **Sticky management revocation.** The orphan reason `device_revoked` means the device record is management-inactive (`devices.is_active = false`, set only by `revoke_device_management`, which no phase-1 path reverses and which also blocks re-pairing); a missing, suspended or replaced pairing alone is the non-orphan reason `device_session_unavailable`. Once derived, `device_revoked` cannot clear through re-pairing. There is no cross-device recording in phase 1; a permanently dead or revoked device's approvals are `orphan_approved` and go to documented provider void / refund reconciliation.
  8. **Contract corrections (the 25 minor findings and six notes).** (a) [MVP_SCOPE](MVP_SCOPE.md) §5 gains one exact exception: the §4.33 audit registry, safe-detail labels and ar / he / en action titles that 001D ships, in the files the packet lists — no analytics or report redesign; (b) `method='card'` is classified correctly on the intended tender surfaces, while the all-tender "Cash collected" label and the sales-summary fallback are separate defects of `DASHBOARD-COLLECTED-LABEL-001`, and the production LIVE gate requires that ticket deployed or a recorded owner acceptance of the mislabel; (c) a TEST provider identity is server-refused (point 1); (d) the DEPLOYMENT §18.2 PRE / POST covers the source, hash and ACL of every function 001D re-creates; (e) Q-048 is resolved, or its interim explicitly accepted by the command room, before LIVE; (f) and (g) bounded Resume evidence and retry authority (point 3); (h) the claim guard first applies exactly the payload-independent part of the dispatched RPC's own authorization — membership, role, capability, own-shift and device-type rules — and defers only when that RPC is certain to deny, never on a check of its own (reading the session's membership row FOR SHARE and deciding on that locked version), so only an operation whose session that RPC admits can receive `payment_in_progress` (the RPC's payload-dependent denials come after the guard), and its refusals carry no attempt identifier, so a memoized replay never discloses one (this also answers the review's note on replay envelopes); (i) `card_attempt_mark_recording_result` proves the sync row is the attempt's own `payment.create` card leg of the same order and stores one closed token list (any other token as `nonrecoverable_refusal`), so a manual-card `card_terminal_required` row is never attached to a provider attempt; (j) the attested-approver wording (point 6); (k) a resolved row is immutable — any provider fact that still reaches it (only a reply already in flight can) writes only the private event `security.card_resolution_contradicted`, which the operator reviews — and a change of anomaly code, `charge_may_exist` or classification, or a `release_failed` refusal code, is audited while a timestamp- or counter-only write is not; (l) the never-delivered leg (point 2); (m) the LIVE activation gates (point 4); (n) sticky revocation (point 7); (o) the strict `recorded` join (point 1); (p) the `recording_resume` key scope and shape — the review's two findings on it (point 3); (q) for card-sensitive operations the SQLSTATE classes `08`, `40`, `53`, `55`, `57`, `58` and `XX` are transient — never a permanent provider-recording refusal — except the dispatched RPC's own raises under the exact prefix allowlist `record_payment:` / `close_shift:`; (r) a commit approval requires a tip absent or exactly zero, and a late commit reply finishes only a still-`sent` row; (s) the configuration environment never switches while an attempt of either mode still needs it for a status lookup (a `sent`, `unknown` or `released` attempt without a `resolution`, one freed by `free_order` included), and the kill switch stops new starts without switching it; (t) a TEST revision requires a positive, bounded `max_test_amount_minor` (fail closed); (u) a TEST private event can be held on its own and the purge skips held events; (v) `free_order` proves the session and a `manager`+ role before any attempt lookup and writes mode-aware denial evidence only after resolving the attempt; (w) TEST attempts store and project no card or provider-reference data — they keep the state, the return code and class, the approval type, the status code, the anomaly code and `charge_may_exist`, the amount and currency, the correlation identity and their lookup, release, hold, retention and time metadata (amending **D-053** point 4); (x) reference fields follow a conservative ASCII allowlist with a length cap, free-text reasons are printable, trimmed and bounded, both reject a 12–19-digit Luhn-valid run and any run of 20 or more digits, digits counted across spaces, hyphens, underscores, dots and slashes (helper and CHECK, on the ledger's reference and note fields and on the private events' `reason`, `actor_reference` and `incident_hold_reference`), and the tenant audit stores reason-code tokens only. Notes: the 001D exact-scope authorization is tied to the eventual #363 merge commit, never #362 alone; pgTAP proves database mutation and call-intent conditions, the Edge fake-provider lane proves provider-call counts and the Dart lane UI and transport behaviour; the documented lock graph includes the proof's identity-row locks and `record_payment`'s post-counter locks, with a printer-only stress case, and the in-dispatch proofs read identity rows without row locks; `BIZBOT-PAYMENTS-ZCREDIT-PILOT-001H-PILOT-OPS` owns the read-only Card Attempts incident view that consumes `owner_card_attempts` — a documented read-only query that an in-scope `org_owner`, `restaurant_owner`, `manager` or `accountant` runs in their own authenticated session (never another person's, **D-004**), with no new app surface (a Dashboard screen would need its own Work ID and an MVP_SCOPE §5 exception); the completed-payment and fully-settled checks run as separate statements after `orders` FOR UPDATE returns.
- **Alternatives considered:** checking a provider identity only while its claim is active — rejected (the review's fall-through records a freed or TEST charge); keeping `expected_revision` on the recording leg — rejected (any kitchen status step orphans an approval); authorizing Resume on role — rejected (revocation leaves the role in place); direct DML for the mapping — rejected (unattributable, no one-device invariant); relying on the provider's duplicate check for a resent `start` — rejected (**Q-049** unproven); calling the operator actor "verified" — rejected (nothing authenticates the owner an SQL session names); leaving a TEST attempt that no lookup resolves to the 90-day purge — rejected (it would block the TEST row's retirement, the LIVE activation and the environment switch for months, and forever under a hold).
- **Consequences:** (+) no provider identity can become revenue outside its own admissible leg; kitchen movement never orphans an approval; revoked actors lose Resume; the till binding is attributable and single-device; a resent `start` cannot re-charge; operator evidence states exactly what it proves. (−) the guard gains the identity-first branch; 001F carries a provider-leg sibling record; the mapping gains a lifecycle and an operator RPC; private events gain the executor principal; new typed tokens `provider_recording_refused`, `test_attempt_not_recordable`, `resume_authority_required`, `actor_not_active` and `device_session_unavailable`; the operator resolution gains the TEST-only `test_closed`. **RISK R-002**, **R-003**, **R-007** and **R-008** narrowed.
- **Related:** **D-051** (points 2 and 3), **D-052** (points 3, 6 and 7), **D-053** (points 2, 4, 6, 7 and 9), **D-054** (points 4–10 and the alternatives), **D-055** (point 5), **D-056** (points 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10) — each carries a dated 2026-10-23 note; [API_CONTRACT](API_CONTRACT.md) §4.7, §4.14, §4.30b, §4.33, §4.35 item 4, §4.55; [DOMAIN_MODEL](DOMAIN_MODEL.md) §8.4–§8.6; [STATE_MACHINES](STATE_MACHINES.md) §5, §10; [OFFLINE_SYNC_SPEC](OFFLINE_SYNC_SPEC.md) §8, §10.1; [MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC](MONEY_AND_TAX_SPEC.md) §1, §11, §12.3, §17, §19; [OPEN_QUESTIONS](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) Q-048, Q-057; [SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL](SECURITY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md) §12, TH-8, T-023; [TESTING_STRATEGY](TESTING_STRATEGY.md) §6; [RUNBOOKS](RUNBOOKS.md) §8; [DEPLOYMENT](DEPLOYMENT.md) §18; [OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY](OPERATIONS_AND_RECOVERY.md); [MVP_SCOPE](MVP_SCOPE.md) §5; [BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001](BIZBOT_PAYMENTS_ZCREDIT_PILOT_001.md).
